- [2026-05-23 20:35 UTC+05:30] **Runner no longer sends `temperature` to OpenAI's Responses API for codex-family models (`gpt-5-codex`, `gpt-5.1-codex`, dated snapshots), which previously broke `self-improver` and any agent pinned to a codex model with HTTP 400.** `translateToResponsesRequest` in [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) now gates `body.temperature` on `!modelRequiresResponsesApi(model)` so codex/reasoning models that reject the parameter are omitted while non-codex models forced onto the Responses path via `LLM_USE_RESPONSES_API=1` still receive it. Tests updated in [scripts/agent-runner-responses-api.test.mjs](scripts/agent-runner-responses-api.test.mjs).

- [2026-05-23 18:30 UTC+05:30] **Onboarding tour tooltip now follows the viewport on scroll/resize so the Next/Back/Skip controls are always reachable without scrolling.** [apps/web/src/components/onboarding/tour-tooltip.tsx](apps/web/src/components/onboarding/tour-tooltip.tsx) previously rendered the tooltip card with `position: absolute` and computed `top`/`left` once (after a 350ms delay) using `window.scrollY` / `window.scrollX`, so any user scroll after placement left the tooltip — including the Next/Skip buttons — stranded off-screen; the spotlight ring also drifted because its rect was read directly inside JSX and only refreshed on React re-renders. Four changes: (a) Switched the tooltip card from `absolute` to `fixed` and dropped `window.scrollX/Y` from `getTooltipPosition` and `clampPosition` so all coordinates are viewport-relative. (b) Track spotlight rect in React state (`spotlightRect: {top, left, width, height} | null`) and render the ring from state instead of a per-render `getBoundingClientRect()` call. (c) Extracted a `recompute()` callback that walks placement candidates `[preferred, opposite, bottom, top, right, left]`, picks the first that fits within `EDGE_PAD=8` of every viewport edge, falls back to a centered-near-bottom position when the target is fully scrolled off-screen, and clamps to the viewport when no placement fits cleanly — so tall targets like `data-tour="positions"` and the sticky `data-tour="deposit-panel"` always leave the tooltip fully on-screen. (d) Added rAF-throttled `scroll` (passive, capture so nested scroll containers also fire), `resize`, and `ResizeObserver` listeners (on both the target and the tooltip itself) that re-run `recompute()` continuously while a step is active; cleanup tears all four down. The existing `target.scrollIntoView({block: "center"})` call per step is preserved, and three settle timers at 50/200/400ms repaint after the smooth scroll lands.

- [2026-05-23 17:40 UTC+05:30] **Testnet banner now stays sticky on the landing page.** The `.testnet-banner-landing` rule in [apps/web/src/app/globals.css](apps/web/src/app/globals.css) set `position: relative` purely to give its decorative `::after` gradient a containing block — but that declaration also overrode the Tailwind `sticky` class on [apps/web/src/components/layout/testnet-banner.tsx](apps/web/src/components/layout/testnet-banner.tsx) when `pathname === "/"`. The banner scrolled away with the page while the header kept its `top: var(--testnet-banner-h)` 28px offset, leaving an empty 28px strip above the sticky header. Dropped the `position: relative` override (sticky already establishes a containing block for the absolutely positioned `::after` gradient) and left a comment so this regression can't be reintroduced. The non-landing routes were already correct via `position: sticky` from Tailwind.

- [2026-05-23 17:38 UTC+05:30] **Hero "Live testnet stats" badge now uses the amber palette of the testnet warning banner instead of the red live-alert palette.** [apps/web/src/components/primer/HeroStats.tsx](apps/web/src/components/primer/HeroStats.tsx) swaps the border / background / text / pulse-dot from `red-500` / `app-accent` / `app-border` to `amber-500/30` / `amber-500/10` / `amber-700` (`amber-300` in dark mode), matching [apps/web/src/components/layout/testnet-banner.tsx](apps/web/src/components/layout/testnet-banner.tsx) so users read the badge as a testnet-caution indicator rather than a "live alert".

- [2026-05-23 08:50 UTC+05:30] **Self-improver issue opener no longer fails with `description is too long (maximum is 100 characters)` → `could not add label: 'agent-finding' not found` cascade.** The `agent-finding` label description in [apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.js) was 105 chars, over GitHub's documented 100-char cap, so `gh label create` returned HTTP 422 and `ensureLabelsExist` soft-fail-warned without aborting; every subsequent `gh issue create --label agent-finding` then cascaded into the "label not found" red herring, hiding the real `gh` stderr in CI. Three hardening changes: (a) Shortened the description to 99 chars (`Issue surfaced by self-improver-issues agent or human via .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml.`). (b) Added an exported `GITHUB_LABEL_DESCRIPTION_MAX = 100` constant and a module-load invariant that throws on import if any `ISSUE_LABELS` / `PR_LABELS` entry exceeds it — regression caught at unit-test time, not in CI. Plus a defensive `truncateLabelDescription(s)` helper and matching truncation + `console.warn` inside `buildLabelCreateArgs` for runtime callers that hand-roll specs. (c) Both opener scripts now hard-fail BEFORE shelling out to `gh issue create` / `gh pr create` when a label they will reference failed to bootstrap, instead of soft-fail-warning: [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs) exports new pure helpers `requiredIssueLabelNames(proposals)` (the static `agent-finding` / `needs-human-review` plus the dynamic `category:<x>` per proposal) and `labelBootstrapFailures({ensureResults, requiredNames})`, returning `{ok:false, error_code: "LABEL_BOOTSTRAP_FAILED", error: "gh label create failed for required label(s): <name> — <real gh stderr>"}` when the bootstrap stripe blocks the upcoming filing; [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs) does the same with `requiredPrLabelNames()` (the two PR labels `openPr` attaches). Soft-fail is retained for non-required label failures (e.g. unused `category:*` labels this tick) so transient permission issues on unrelated labels don't block the run. Tests: new [apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.test.mjs](apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.test.mjs) with 9 cases (length cap invariant for both `ISSUE_LABELS` and `PR_LABELS`, exact post-fix description pinned, truncation no-op + clip + nullish + defensive truncation in `buildLabelCreateArgs`); 5 new cases in [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.test.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.test.mjs) and 4 new cases in [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs) pin the required-label set computation and the gh-stderr-passthrough behaviour.

- [2026-05-23 08:50 UTC+05:30] **Self-improver PR opener no longer silently ships manifests with cross-edit `search`-string interference.** Previously the MCP's `propose_file_edit` validated each new edit's `search` against the *original* on-disk file, but the post-risk-officer applier ([scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs::applyEditsToWorkingTree](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs)) applies the manifest's `edits[]` sequentially against the mutating working tree. So if edit A and edit B in the same manifest both touched the same file and A perturbed the region B's `search` depended on, B passed propose-time and failed apply-time with `SEARCH_NOT_FOUND` — burning the LLM cost and risk-officer turn for a manifest that could never land. Pulled the apply-time invariant earlier: new pure module [apps/mcps/repo-editor/edit-replay.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/edit-replay.js) exports `previewReplaceEdit` (same shape the applier already used, now with `replacementIndex` on errors) and `replayPriorEdits({manifest, targetPath, baseContents})` which replays every prior replace-edit targeting the same path against the file to produce the post-prior-edits scratch buffer. The MCP's `propose_file_edit` handler in [apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js) now reads the manifest first, calls `replayPriorEdits` to compute the scratch state the applier will see, then validates the new edit's `replacements[]` against that scratch buffer; if any prior edit fails to replay (file already drifted out of band), returns `error_code: "PRIOR_EDIT_REPLAY_FAILED"` with the offending edit id so the agent can surface a `## Followups` note instead of stacking another bad edit. The applier's `previewReplaceEdit` is now a thin pass-through to the shared module, so MCP-time and apply-time enforce the exact same invariant. Tests: new [apps/mcps/repo-editor/edit-replay.test.mjs](apps/mcps/repo-editor/edit-replay.test.mjs) with 11 cases (single + multi-replacement happy paths, `SEARCH_NOT_FOUND` / `SEARCH_AMBIGUOUS` rejections with `replacementIndex`, sequential anchor-removal catch, `replayPriorEdits` with no-prior / matching-path / different-path / non-replace-kinds / `PRIOR_EDIT_REPLAY_FAILED`, and an end-to-end cross-edit interference scenario showing the new gate catches what the old propose-time check missed).

- [2026-05-23 07:55 UTC+05:30] **`npm run deploy:local` (and the other `*:local` forge-script flows) no longer fail with `Error: Chain anvil-hardhat not supported` on Foundry 1.5.1.** Supersedes the 2026-05-23 03:40 entry below — that fix kept `chain = 31337` in the `[etherscan].local` block of [foundry.toml](foundry.toml) on the theory that the chain id needed to round-trip through Foundry's Etherscan trace identifier. With the Etherscan v2 client now wired in (foundry-rs/foundry#10893), that `chain = 31337` field is actually validated against v2's hard list of supported chain IDs whenever `ETHERSCAN_API_KEY` is present in the process environment (Foundry auto-loads `.env`, so any developer with a real Etherscan key set for sepolia/arbitrum hits this on every `forge script --broadcast`). 31337 (`anvil-hardhat`) isn't on the v2 list, so forge bails with the new error before broadcasting any tx. Fix: drop `chain = 31337` from the `local` entry — the bare `local = { key = "anvil-noop", url = "http://127.0.0.1:8545/verify" }` still matches `--rpc-url local` and lets the trace identifier resolve via the alias without going through the v2 chain-support check; the URL is never contacted because no `*:local` flow passes `--verify`. Comment in `foundry.toml` rewritten to document both the v1-era (`No known Etherscan API URL`) and v2-era (`Chain anvil-hardhat not supported`) failure modes so a future agent doesn't reintroduce the `chain` field. Verified by running `PATH=/Users/reuben/.foundry/bin:$PATH npm run deploy:local` against a fresh `anvil --chain-id 31337` — script exits 0 and writes a fresh `apps/web/src/config/local-deployment.json`. `sync:local` also clears the etherscan check (separate `Insufficient funds for gas` is unrelated; that script uses the `.env` `PRIVATE_KEY` which has no balance on a fresh Anvil — pre-existing).

- [2026-05-23 07:55 UTC+05:30] **`apps/web/playwright.config.ts` now spawns its E2E Next.js dev server on a dedicated port (default `3010`, override with `E2E_PORT`) and forwards `NEXT_PUBLIC_ENVIO_URL` from `process.env` to that server, so a developer can keep `npm run dev` running on `:3000` against production Envio (per their `apps/web/.env.local`) while Playwright tests hit a separate dev server with the local Envio URL injected.** Previously the config hard-coded port 3000 and only forwarded `NEXT_PUBLIC_E2E_TEST_MODE` + `NEXT_PUBLIC_PRIVY_APP_ID`, which meant (a) running E2E against a local Envio required editing `.env.local` and bouncing the dev server, and (b) `reuseExistingServer: true` would silently bind to whatever was already on 3000 with whatever Envio URL it was started with. Forward list now driven by a small `E2E_FORWARDED_ENV` whitelist (`NEXT_PUBLIC_PRIVY_APP_ID`, `NEXT_PUBLIC_ENVIO_URL`); empty-string values are passed through verbatim so callers can *override* a non-empty value in `.env.local` (e.g. `NEXT_PUBLIC_PRIVY_APP_ID= playwright test ...` forces the wagmi mock-connector path that the `cross_chain_e2e` CI job uses without Privy secrets). Caveat: Next.js 16's dev lockfile at `.next/dev/lock` still serializes `next dev` per project directory, so a developer running `npm run dev` on `:3000` and Playwright spawning `next dev` on `:3010` will collide on the lock — you have to stop the dev server before running E2E. Documented for the next pass; addressing it requires a different runtime (e.g. `next build` + `next start`) or a per-test-run project copy.

- [2026-05-23 07:50 UTC+05:30] **Self-improver issue and PR openers now auto-create the GitHub labels they reference**, fixing the `could not add label: 'agent-finding' not found` failure that aborted [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs) in fresh repos. New shared spec module [apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/agent-labels.js) exports `ISSUE_LABELS` (`agent-finding`, `needs-human-review`, and one `category:<x>` label per `CATEGORY_ENUM` entry — matching `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml` verbatim), `PR_LABELS` (`agent-self-improvement`, `needs-human-review`), and a pure `buildLabelCreateArgs(label)` helper. Both [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs) and [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs) gain a small `ensureLabelsExist({labels})` helper that idempotently `gh label create --force`s every spec (with colour + description) before calling `gh issue create` / `gh pr create`. `--force` updates the label in-place if it already exists, so the bootstrap is safe to re-run every tick; per-label failures soft-fail with a `console.warn` instead of throwing so a permissions hiccup on labels (which may exist already) doesn't abort the whole opener. The issue-opener's bootstrap is gated on `cap_filter.kept.length > 0` so empty manifests don't churn labels needlessly. Two new drift-guard tests assert every label name `buildGhCreateArgs` / `openPr` emits (`agent-finding`, `needs-human-review`, every `category:<CATEGORY_ENUM entry>`, `agent-self-improvement`) is present in the corresponding spec — catches a missing label spec at test time if a future `CATEGORY_ENUM` value lands without the matching `ISSUE_LABELS` entry. Plus one argv-shape pin for `buildLabelCreateArgs` and validators that every spec declares non-empty `name`, 6-char hex `color`, and non-empty `description`. All 38 tests across [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.test.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.test.mjs) + [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs) pass.

### Added

- [2026-05-23 07:10 UTC+05:30] **GitHub PR + issue templates for humans and agents at [.github/pull_request_template.md](.github/pull_request_template.md) and [.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/) (config.yml, bug.yml, feature.yml, agent-finding.yml), and aligned the self-improver's agent-authored bodies to the same heading structure so humans and agents file structurally identical PRs and issues.** Background: the self-improver meta-loop already opens PRs (via `scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs::buildPrBody`) and the MCP scaffolding to file issues exists (`apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js::formatIssueBody`), but humans had no template at all and the agent-authored bodies followed a one-off shape that wasn't grep-friendly for reviewers. Five files + two formatter updates:
  - **[.github/pull_request_template.md](.github/pull_request_template.md)** with eight sections — Summary, Type of change (checklist incl. `Agent-authored self-improvement`), Linked issues, Test plan (per-surface checklist: `forge fmt --check`, `forge test`, `npm run lint:web`, Vitest, `test:e2e:ci`, push-worker, keeper), Risk + rollback (blast radius + rollback path), Docs + ABIs + changelog (cites `.cursor/rules/docs-sync.mdc`, `.cursor/rules/changelog-updates.mdc`, and the [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) ABI regen policy), Agent metadata (HTML-comment block agents fill in: `agent`, `runId`, `manifestPath`, `requiresReviewKind`, `convictionWeight`, `riskOfficerVerdict`, trigger signals), and Reviewer checklist (re-run `apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs --apply-locally-only`, no secrets touched).
  - **[.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/config.yml)** disables blank issues and routes security disclosures to `security@indexflow.app` and operator questions to `docs/README.md` + `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md` via `contact_links`.
  - **[.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml)** form: summary, repro, expected/actual, multi-select `affected_surface` (solidity-contracts, apps/web, apps/push-worker, apps/envio, services/keeper, agent-runner, mcp-servers, infra/ci, docs, other), `network` (local/sepolia/fuji/mainnet-future/n/a), tx/addr, logs (shell-rendered), environment, and a checkbox requiring the reporter to confirm it isn't a security vulnerability. Auto-labels `bug` + `triage`.
  - **[.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/feature.yml](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/feature.yml)** form: problem statement, proposal, affected_surface, acceptance criteria, alternatives considered, risks/open questions. Auto-labels `enhancement` + `triage`.
  - **[.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml)** form schema-locked to [apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js): `category` dropdown matches `CATEGORY_ENUM` (`new_mcp_or_skill`, `strategy_idea`, `data_gap`, `refactor`, `investigation`) verbatim; `conviction_weight` dropdown spans `0.1`–`1.0` matching the `clampConviction` clamp; `agent_name` + `manifest_id` round-trip fields; footer template with the `<!-- self-improver-issue-id: __MANIFEST_ID__ -->` marker so human-filed `agent-finding` issues dedupe against agent-filed ones via `extractIssueIdMarker`. Auto-labels `agent-finding` + `needs-human-review`.
  - **`buildPrBody` in [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs)** now emits the same eight top-level sections as the PR template (Summary, Type of change, Linked issues, Test plan, Risk + rollback, Docs + ABIs + changelog, Agent metadata, Reviewer checklist), auto-ticks `[x] Agent-authored self-improvement` and additionally `[x] Infra / CI / build` when any edit has `requiresReviewKind` ∈ {`runner`, `mcp`, `shared`}, computes blast-radius copy from the rollup of `requiresReviewKind` values, and surfaces the strongest `convictionWeight` across edits. The historical sub-sections (Trigger signals, Proposed edits, Justifications, Risk officer verdict, Housekeeping rotations, Files touched) move under `## Agent metadata` so existing CI greps for those headings keep working. Tests in [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs) gained 2 new cases pinning the runner/mcp/shared blast-radius copy and the strongest-conviction rollup; all 11 pass.
  - **`formatIssueBody` in [apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js)** reorders its body to match the agent-finding form (Category → Summary → Agent name → Justification → Conviction → Trigger signals → marker footer), adds an optional `agentName` parameter for the eventual `apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs` applier to thread through, and preserves `ISSUE_ID_MARKER_PREFIX` verbatim so dedupe via `extractIssueIdMarker` is unchanged. Tests in [apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.test.mjs](apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.test.mjs) gained 3 new cases pinning field order, the optional Agent name line, and the empty-signals path; all 20 pass.
  - **`scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs`** + **`scripts/run-self-improvement-issue-risk-officer.mjs`** + agent prompts ([agents/self-improver-issues.md](agents/self-improver-issues.md), [agents/risk-officer-self-improvement-issues.md](agents/risk-officer-self-improvement-issues.md)) flipped from the bespoke `agent-self-improvement-issue` / `needs-human-triage` label pair to the agent-finding template's `agent-finding` / `needs-human-review` labels. Bot-filed issues now also prefix titles with `agent: ` (the same prefix the template auto-applies), and the opener threads `agentName: "self-improver-issues"` into `formatIssueBody` so the rendered body fills in the form's `agent_name` field. Net effect: a `gh issue list --label agent-finding --state open` query returns one unified queue containing both human-filed `agent-finding` form submissions and bot-authored issues — the per-period `MAX_OPEN_SELF_IMPROVER_ISSUES` cap and the title/id-marker dedup catch duplicates across both sources. Round-trip dedupe via the `<!-- self-improver-issue-id: <SHA-12> -->` body marker is unchanged. The label-rename also touches the workflow comment block in [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml), the docs entry in [docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md](docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md), and the `list_open_issues` MCP tool description in [apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js) so every reference reads the same label name.

- [2026-05-23 07:07 UTC+05:30] **Self-improver issues channel: complementary always-on lane that surfaces broader, more speculative improvement ideas as GitHub Issues for human triage, beside the existing PR-only lane that fires only on Layer A signals.** Background: the PR pipeline (added in the entry below) deliberately demands a literal `search`/`replace` diff with ≥2 cited run-log entries, which keeps PR noise low but means observations that aren't yet provable as code edits ("we should wire up an Atlas news MCP", "this vault has a weird PnL pattern, worth a look", "BHP-listed mining names have wider Yahoo symbol resolution gaps than TSXV names") never reach humans. The new channel is additive — the PR pipeline and its allow-list are untouched, both channels live in the same `self-improve` job in [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml) sharing the checkout + concurrency slot. Pipeline:
  - **Layer F — `self-improver-issues` meta-agent at [agents/self-improver-issues.md](agents/self-improver-issues.md)** runs every tick through the existing `node scripts/agent-runner.mjs self-improver-issues` flow (zero runner changes). Frontmatter: `maxTurns: 12`, `temperature: 0.4`, `mcpServers: [repo-editor-mcp]`, `writeTools: [propose_issue]`. System prompt instructs it to consult Layer A signals as **context, not as a gate**, scan the trading agents' run logs for behaviour patterns that aren't yet code-actionable, check open issues for duplicates via `list_open_issues`, then draft 1-3 broader ideas across five categories: `new_mcp_or_skill` / `strategy_idea` / `data_gap` / `refactor` / `investigation`. Lower evidence bar than the PR channel (one run-log citation OR a coherent architectural argument is enough) but never zero. Memory at `agents/memory/self-improver-issues/` (same shape as every other agent), persisted to `main` by the same `commit-results` job that carries the trading agents' memory.
  - **Layer F-MCP — `propose_issue` + `list_open_issues` tools added to [apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/index.js)** (the existing 7 tools stay unchanged). `propose_issue({title, body, category, justification, convictionWeight?})` writes to `.agent-self-improvement/proposed-issues.json` via pure helpers in new [apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.js) (`emptyIssueManifest`, `addIssue`, `findDuplicateIssue`, `dropLowConvictionIssues`, `formatIssueBody`, `extractIssueIdMarker`, constants `MAX_TITLE_CHARS = 120` / `MAX_BODY_CHARS = 8000` / `CATEGORY_ENUM`). Title-hash dedup within the run; the `id` (SHA-12 of the title) is baked into the issue body as `<!-- self-improver-issue-id: <id> -->` as a round-trip key the opener uses to dedup against open issues. `list_open_issues({label?, search?, limit?})` shells out to `gh issue list --json number,title,labels,createdAt,url,body --limit N` (capped at 50) and returns parsed JSON; if `gh` is unauthenticated returns `{available: false, error_code: "GH_NOT_AVAILABLE"}` instead of crashing so the agent can still proceed (the opener's cap-respecting filter catches dups). Neither tool touches the filesystem outside `.agent-self-improvement/`, so the existing repo-editor allow-list rules don't need updating; no new entry in [agents/mcp-servers.json](agents/mcp-servers.json) (same MCP server).
  - **Layer F' — issue risk-officer at [scripts/run-self-improvement-issue-risk-officer.mjs](scripts/run-self-improvement-issue-risk-officer.mjs)** + prompt at [agents/risk-officer-self-improvement-issues.md](agents/risk-officer-self-improvement-issues.md). Mirrors the PR risk-officer pattern with the **same `approve` / `downsize` / `veto` schema** (downsize trims issues whose `convictionWeight < threshold`) but the **rubric is softer** because issues don't change code — the cost of filing a weak one is low (a human closes it) but the cost of vetoing a useful one is high. Approves on at least one run-log citation OR a coherent architectural argument; downsizes batches of 3+ when only some issues clear the bar; vetoes when the per-period cap is full, when an `investigation` issue lacks a target vault address, when titles/ids collide with open issues, or when the same issue id was approved within 24h. Deterministic cap preflight at `checkCapPreflight({openIssueCount, cap, proposalCount})` short-circuits to veto when `openIssueCount >= cap` before any LLM call. Verdict file at `.agent-self-improvement/issue-risk-officer-verdict.json`; recent-verdicts ledger at `.agent-self-improvement/recent-issue-verdicts.json`.
  - **Layer F'' — issue opener at [scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.mjs)** (~250 LOC + 15 tests). Two modes: `--dry-run` prints what would be filed; `--open-issues` dedupes against open issues by id marker AND exact title match via `gh issue list --label agent-finding --json number,title,labels,createdAt,url,body`, respects `MAX_OPEN_SELF_IMPROVER_ISSUES` (default 10) by sorting survivors by conviction DESC and keeping at most `cap - currentOpenCount`, then prefixes every title with `agent: ` and calls `gh issue create --title "agent: <title>" --body <formatIssueBody> --label agent-finding --label needs-human-review --label category:<x>` per survivor. The labels + title prefix match [`.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml`](.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/agent-finding.yml) verbatim so bot-filed and human-filed agent findings share the same triage queue. **Never `--assignee`, never edits or closes existing issues** — pure additive. Body includes a `### Layer A signals active when this issue was drafted` block from the most recent `signal.json`, the conviction + category + justification, and the round-trip id marker comment.
  - **Layer F''' — workflow wiring** appends 4 new steps to the existing `self-improve` job in [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml): `Layer F — run self-improver-issues meta-agent` (`if: always()` so it fires every tick, NOT gated on `steps.signal.outputs.should_run`), `Layer F' — issue risk-officer review`, `Inspect issue risk-officer verdict` (sets `verdict_kind`, `proposal_count`, `dropped_count` outputs), and `Layer F'' — open issues` (gated only on `verdict_kind != 'veto' && verdict_kind != 'missing' && proposal_count != '0'`). Adds `issues: write` to the existing `permissions:` block (`contents: write` + `pull-requests: write` already there for the PR channel) so `gh issue create` works. The existing PR pipeline keeps every `if: steps.signal.outputs.should_run == 'true'` gate untouched. `MAX_OPEN_SELF_IMPROVER_ISSUES=10` exported to both Layer F' and F'' so the cap stays consistent. The existing `self-improver-artefacts` workflow artefact upload now also captures `agents/memory/self-improver-issues/`.
  - **Docs + tests:** [docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md](docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md) section renamed "Self-Improvement (PR + issues meta-loop)" with a new "Self-Improvement issues channel" subsection documenting the five layers, the F-MCP tools, the soft rubric, and local invocation commands. **53 new unit tests** across `apps/mcps/repo-editor/issue-manifest.test.mjs` (17 — title hashing, dedup, conviction clamping, validation, category enum, body formatting round-trip via `extractIssueIdMarker`), `scripts/run-self-improvement-issue-risk-officer.test.mjs` (11 — payload builder, verdict application, cap-preflight veto math, malformed-downsize defaults), `scripts/apply-self-improvement-issues.test.mjs` (15 — cap env parsing, dedup by id marker AND exact title, cap-respecting selection ranked by conviction, `gh issue create` argv shape, body builder identity). All pass via `node --test`. The pure-helper design (file IO + string ops only, `gh` and LLM calls injectable) means the issues pipeline can be exercised end-to-end against a tmp directory without touching `gh`, git, or the network.

- [2026-05-23 06:55 UTC+05:30] **Self-improving meta-loop: after every vault-agent CI tick a new `self-improve` job in [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml) reads accumulated run-log memory and opens a PR with proposed strategy/code edits — never a direct commit to `main`, aligned with the [AGENTS.md](AGENTS.md) "never auto-commit" rule.** Five layers chained together so quiet ticks cost zero LLM tokens and noisy ticks land an auditable PR:
  - **Layer A — deterministic signal detector at [scripts/detect-self-improvement-signal.mjs](scripts/detect-self-improvement-signal.mjs)** (~410 LOC + [scripts/detect-self-improvement-signal.test.mjs](scripts/detect-self-improvement-signal.test.mjs) 19 tests). Pure file-IO walk over `agents/memory/*/run-log.*.jsonl` that emits JSON `{shouldRun, agents[], signals[], housekeeping[]}` for five trigger conditions: `recurring_losers` (same `(ticker, side)` closed at `< -5%` ≥ 2x in 7d), `new_error_code` (MCP `error_code` not seen in prior 100 runs), `cap_saturation` (`maxNewPositionsPerRun` / `maxNewShortsPerRun` errored on ≥ 3 consecutive runs), `risk_officer_dissonance` (≥ 3 risk-officer `veto` verdicts on the same vault in 24h), `loss_streak` (≥ 3 `<-5%` closes in 24h). Also emits housekeeping rotations for run-log entries older than 90 days so the active log stays inside the runner's 5-entry `recentRuns` window indefinitely. When `shouldRun: false` the whole meta-loop short-circuits before any LLM call.
  - **Layer B — `self-improver` meta-agent at [agents/self-improver.md](agents/self-improver.md)** runs unchanged through the existing `node scripts/agent-runner.mjs self-improver` flow (no runner changes needed — inherits memory, dry-run, retry, redaction, confirmation gate for free). Connects to a single new MCP server `repo-editor-mcp` and proposes ≤3 surgical edits per signal via `propose_file_edit` / `propose_file_create` / `propose_file_rename`. Writes the proposal manifest to `.agent-self-improvement/proposed-edits.json` only — **never mutates the repo on disk itself**. Frontmatter: `maxTurns: 20`, `temperature: 0.1`, `mcpServers: [repo-editor-mcp]`. The runner persists its `## Thesis` to `agents/memory/self-improver/state.json` so the next run knows what was diagnosed previously.
  - **Layer B' — `repo-editor-mcp` MCP server at [apps/mcps/repo-editor/](apps/mcps/repo-editor/)** (~410 LOC across `index.js` / `allowlist.js` / `proposal-manifest.js` + 39 tests across `allowlist.test.mjs` + `proposal-manifest.test.mjs`). Six tools: `get_self_improvement_signals` (shells out to Layer A), `list_run_log({agent, network, limit})`, `read_repo_file({path, sliceStart?, sliceLength?})` (gated by the allow-list, truncates at 256 KB), `propose_file_edit({path, replacements[], justification, convictionWeight?})` (validates every `search` is present-and-unique in the live file BEFORE recording — defence against hallucinated diffs), `propose_file_create`, `propose_file_rename`, `summarize_proposals`. **Allowlist at [apps/mcps/repo-editor/allowlist.js](apps/mcps/repo-editor/allowlist.js)** is the load-bearing safety rail: allows `agents/*.md`, `agents/skills/*.md`, `agents/mcp-servers.json`, `scripts/agent-runner*.{mjs,js}` (flagged `requiresReviewKind=runner`), `apps/mcps/**/*.{mjs,js}` (flagged `requiresReviewKind=mcp`), `apps/shared/**/*.{mjs,js}`. Denies (always) every `*.sol`, `lib/**`, `src/**`, `script/**`, `test/**`, `.github/workflows/**`, `.github/actions/**`, generated ABIs (`apps/web/src/abi/**`, `apps/envio/abis/**`), `apps/web/src/config/*-deployment.json`, every `.env*` / credentials / secret file, governance docs (`AGENTS.md`, `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `.cursor/rules/**`), runner-owned data (`agents/memory/**`, `apps/web/public/agent-metadata/**`), `node_modules/**`, `.git/**`, lockfiles. Default-deny for anything not on the allow-list. The PR-opener (Layer D) re-checks the same allow-list before any disk mutation so a bug in one layer can't widen the blast radius.
  - **Layer C — risk-officer pass at [scripts/run-self-improvement-risk-officer.mjs](scripts/run-self-improvement-risk-officer.mjs)** + prompt at [agents/risk-officer-self-improvement.md](agents/risk-officer-self-improvement.md). Reuses the `parseRiskOfficerVerdict` parser pattern from the existing trade-batch risk officer, but adapts the verdict shape to `{verdict, reason, downsizeThreshold?}` where `downsize` drops every edit whose `convictionWeight < threshold`. Payload includes the manifest, Layer A signals, the CURRENT contents of every touched file (so the officer can verify proposed `search` strings actually exist — a hallucinated diff is an automatic veto), the allow-list rule each edit matched, and the last 10 self-improver verdicts (so the officer doesn't re-approve the same PR twice across ticks). 8 unit tests in [scripts/run-self-improvement-risk-officer.test.mjs](scripts/run-self-improvement-risk-officer.test.mjs) pin `applyVerdictToManifest` (approve / veto / downsize with valid threshold / downsize with malformed threshold defaults to approve / null verdict defensive-approve) + `buildRiskOfficerUserPayload` (sections complete, new-file contents stubbed to `<N char new file>`) + the empty-manifest fast path (no LLM call).
  - **Layer D — PR opener at [scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs](scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.mjs)** (~420 LOC + 9 tests). Two modes: `--apply-locally-only` re-validates the manifest against the allow-list, applies the deterministic search/replace + create + rename edits to the working tree, and applies the housekeeping rotations from Layer A. `--open-pr` additionally creates a stable `agent-improve/<UTC date>-<signal-hash>` branch (two ticks firing the same signal set on the same UTC day collapse onto one branch — no PR spam), commits + pushes under the `vault-agent[bot]` identity (same as the `commit-results` job), and runs `gh pr create --label agent-self-improvement --label needs-human-review --base main`. Dedup via `gh pr list --head <branch> --state open` before `pr create`. **Never `--merge`** — humans review and merge. PR body includes the trigger signals, the proposal table with `(id, kind, path, conviction, requiresReviewKind)`, every justification, the risk-officer verdict + reason, the housekeeping rotation summary, and the full touched-paths list.
  - **Layer E — CI workflow wiring** adds a `self-improve` job to [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml) that depends on `commit-results` (so the freshly-pushed run-log is on `main` when the detector reads it). Steps: Layer A signal detection → meta-agent run (skipped if no signal) → risk-officer review → verdict inspection → `--apply-locally-only` + dry-run replay of every touched agent (`AGENT_DRY_RUN=1` so the LLM loop runs against the new prompt with all on-chain writes no-op'd — catches a prompt that parses but errors on first MCP call BEFORE the PR opens) → `--open-pr`. Runs in a separate `concurrency: agent-self-improver` group so two ticks can't race on the same branch push. `permissions: contents: write + pull-requests: write` for the feature branch + `gh pr create`; the keeper key is intentionally NOT exported here (the meta-loop has no `vault-manager-mcp` access). Skipped on ad-hoc `workflow_dispatch` runs with `dry_run=true` or `vault_address` set. Per-tick scratch dir (`.agent-self-improvement/`) is gitignored and uploaded as the `self-improver-artefacts` workflow artefact (14-day retention) for audit.
  - **MCP registry update at [agents/mcp-servers.json](agents/mcp-servers.json)** registers `repo-editor-mcp` with `envPassthrough: ["AGENT_NAME"]`. **Docs update at [docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md](docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md)** adds a new "Self-Improvement (PR-only meta-loop)" section documenting the five layers, the allow/deny list, and local invocation commands. **`.gitignore` adds `.agent-self-improvement/`** so the per-tick scratch dir never leaks into commits.
  - **Tests:** 45 new pure-helper tests across `scripts/detect-self-improvement-signal.test.mjs` (19), `apps/mcps/repo-editor/allowlist.test.mjs` (26), `apps/mcps/repo-editor/proposal-manifest.test.mjs` (14), `scripts/run-self-improvement-risk-officer.test.mjs` (8), `scripts/apply-self-improvement-proposals.test.mjs` (9). All pass locally via `node --test`. The pure design (file IO + string ops only, LLM call injectable) means the meta-loop can be exercised end-to-end against a tmp directory without touching git, gh, or the network.

- [2026-05-23 06:11 UTC+05:30] **Risk-officer second-pass vets every proposed write batch with an independent LLM call, returning `approve` / `downsize` / `veto` with a structured reason that is persisted alongside the action in `agent-metadata/<vault>.json::recentActions[]` (Quick Win 2 from [trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md](.cursor/plans/trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md)).** Background: in CI (`AGENT_NON_INTERACTIVE_WRITE_EXECUTE=1`) every proposed write fires blind — there is no second opinion between "the LLM emitted a tool call" and "`cast send` broadcasts the transaction", and the mining-manager run log shows a half-finished basket from `wire_asset` failures because no review caught that the wire batch was malformed before it ran. New layer slots in *between* the write-batch classifier and `executeToolCall` without forking the main agent:
  - **New role prompt at [agents/risk-officer.md](agents/risk-officer.md)** — a non-MCP, system-prompt-only file (no `mcpServers`, no `writeTools`) loaded once per run by the new `loadRiskOfficerPrompt()` helper at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) line ~902. Body instructs the officer to read `writeBatch + vaultSnapshot + recentClosedPositions + marketRegime` and emit STRICT JSON: `{verdict: "approve"|"downsize"|"veto", reason: string, downsizeFactor?: number in (0,1]}`. Falls back to `DEFAULT_RISK_OFFICER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` if the file is missing or unparseable, so a broken edit can't take the agent down.
  - **Five new pure helpers at [scripts/agent-runner-confirmation.mjs](scripts/agent-runner-confirmation.mjs)**: `DEFAULT_RISK_OFFICER_SYSTEM_PROMPT` (~70 lines covering the JSON contract + decision rubric), `buildRiskOfficerUserPayload({writeBatch, vaultSnapshot, recentClosedPositions, marketRegime})` (normalises the batch to `{tool, args, justification}`, caps closures at the most-recent 5 so the prompt stays bounded), `parseRiskOfficerVerdict(raw)` (extracts the first balanced JSON object even if the LLM wraps it in prose, validates the discriminated union, rejects `downsize` without a `(0,1]` factor), `applyRiskOfficerDownsize(writeCalls, factor)` (scales `args.collateral` AND `args.size` by the factor on every `open_position` call so leverage stays constant — no-op on non-`open_position` tools like `wire_asset`, no-op on invalid factors, returns `{adjustedCalls, audit[]}` so the per-call `{tool, beforeCollateral, afterCollateral, downsizeFactor}` is persistable), and `runRiskOfficerPass({writeBatch, vaultSnapshot, recentClosedPositions, marketRegime, systemPrompt, llmCall})` (orchestrates the call with an injectable `llmCall` so unit tests don't need a live OpenAI key; defaults to `approve` on empty batches, missing `llmCall`, LLM errors, or unparseable replies — fail-open by design so a risk-officer outage can't brick all writes).
  - **Runner integration at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) lines ~3794-3858** wraps `runRiskOfficerForBatch(writeCalls)` (a closure that pulls `recentClosedPositions` from the current run + last 10 entries of `recentRuns`, best-effort fresh `get_perp_capital_snapshot` with cached fallback, the run's `marketRegime`, and the loaded prompt) around every write batch. On `veto`, the runner calls `pushRejectedToolResponses(...)` with the reason and pushes a user-role nudge ("Risk officer VETOED ... revise your plan and propose an alternative batch — do not retry verbatim") so the LLM gets actionable feedback for the next turn. On `downsize`, every `open_position` in the batch is scaled in place. On `approve`, the batch passes through. Every verdict is pushed onto `policyRuntime.riskOfficerVerdicts[]` and the per-batch verdict is stashed in `policyRuntime.pendingRiskOfficerVerdict` so `executeToolCall` can stamp it onto `runSummary.writeActions[].riskOfficer` for the matching tool call (verdict + reason + `downsizeFactor` + per-call `beforeCollateral`/`afterCollateral` audit). The verdicts roll up into `recentActions[]` published to `agent-metadata/<vault>.json` and into `riskOfficerVerdicts[]` on the run-log entry for full diagnostic context.
  - **`AGENT_RISK_OFFICER` env flag** (defaults ON; `0`/`false`/`no` to disable) gates the entire pass. Also skipped on `DRY_RUN`, on `skipWritesThisBatch`, and when the batch contains no write calls. Cost-bounded: one extra completion per turn that proposes writes, `temperature=0`, no tool definitions on the call (text-only output).
  - **Tests:** new [scripts/agent-runner-risk-officer.test.mjs](scripts/agent-runner-risk-officer.test.mjs) covers the helpers with 16 cases — payload-normalisation + closure cap, all three verdict-parse paths (approve / downsize-with-valid-factor / veto), `downsize` factor validation (rejects `0`, negatives, `>1`, missing), prose-wrapped JSON extraction, garbage rejection, downsize scaling preserves leverage (collateral + size both scaled), downsize ignores non-`open_position` calls, downsize is a no-op on invalid factors, `runRiskOfficerPass` defaults to approve when `llmCall` is missing / throws / returns unparseable text, passes `veto` through unchanged, and short-circuits empty batches without calling the LLM. Full suite: **302/302 tests pass** across `scripts/*.test.mjs` + `apps/mcps/**/*.test.mjs` + `apps/shared/*.test.mjs`.

- [2026-05-23 06:10 UTC+05:30] **`get_market_regime` yfinance tool + conviction-weighted sizing in `plan_open_position` + runner-side regime gate on shorts (Quick Win 3 from [trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md](.cursor/plans/trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md)).** Background: every long-short decision was made in isolation from the underlying commodity tape, so mining-manager would open shorts on weak names even when XME was +5% on the day (a guaranteed squeeze), and `plan_open_position` was splitting `availableCollateral` equally across slots regardless of whether the slot was an ML score 98 or a borderline 85 — leaving the highest-conviction picks under-sized. Three parts:
  - **Pure regime classifier at [apps/shared/market-regime.mjs](apps/shared/market-regime.mjs)** exporting `REGIME_COMPONENT_SYMBOLS = ["GC=F", "HG=F", "XME", "GDX", "DX-Y.NYB"]` (gold, copper, US miners ETF, gold miners ETF, USD index) and `classifyMarketRegime({components})`. Returns `{regime: "metals_risk_on"|"metals_risk_off"|"metals_neutral", components, shortPenalty: 0|1|2, longBonus: 0|1|2, summary}` from each symbol's day-change %. Rules: `metals_risk_on` when ≥3 of 5 components agree (miners up / USD down); `shortPenalty=2` when XME or GDX day-change ≥ `+3.0%` (squeeze risk), `longBonus=2` when both ETFs ≤ `-3.0%` (capitulation). Fully covered by [apps/shared/market-regime.test.mjs](apps/shared/market-regime.test.mjs).
  - **`get_market_regime` tool at [apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js](apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js) line ~289** — fetches day-change for the five `REGIME_COMPONENT_SYMBOLS` in parallel, runs `classifyMarketRegime`, and returns the classification verbatim. The runner pre-fetches this once per run before the LLM loop (no LLM tool call needed, no token cost) and injects a `## Today's Metals Regime` block into the system prompt at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) lines ~1719-1748 with the components, the verdict, and explicit guidance — `shortPenalty=2 → "the runner will auto-block every new short open_position this run with SHORT_BLOCKED_BY_REGIME"`, `longBonus=2 → "miners deeply red on the day; consider upweighting long convictionWeights on top-quartile picks"`.
  - **`plan_open_position` accepts `convictionWeight` + `totalConvictionWeight` at [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) lines ~948-1104**. When both are provided, per-slot collateral becomes `available * convictionWeight / totalConvictionWeight` instead of `available / numNewSlots`. Validation: both must be supplied together (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` otherwise), `convictionWeight > 0` (drop the slot from the batch instead of passing zero), and `totalConvictionWeight ≥ convictionWeight` (it's the SUM across this turn's batch including the current slot). Equal-weight remains the default when both are omitted — no breaking change for `mining-manager` / `quality-matrix-manager` which still emit `equal_weight` sizing. The response payload gains `sizingMode`, `convictionWeight`, and `totalConvictionWeight` so the audit trail is preserved.
  - **Runner-side regime gate** in `validatePolicyWriteBatch` at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) lines ~1581-1583 — every new short `open_position` in a proposed batch is rejected with `SHORT_BLOCKED_BY_REGIME` when `marketRegime.shortPenalty >= 2`. The rejection includes the offending tickers and the regime summary as feedback to the LLM so it doesn't blind-retry the same batch.
  - **Skill docs** at [agents/skills/yfinance.md](agents/skills/yfinance.md) document the new tool + the `shortPenalty` rules so agents that read the skill file know to consult the regime line before proposing shorts; [agents/skills/vault-manager.md](agents/skills/vault-manager.md) `plan_open_position` row documents the conviction-weight parameters.

- [2026-05-23 06:09 UTC+05:30] **Closed-position post-mortems + "## Lessons" prompt block — every agent now re-reads its top-3 wins / top-3 losses from the last 30 days before each decision (Quick Win 1 from [trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md](.cursor/plans/trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md)).** Background: `state.json` carried a single `thesis` string and the run-log carried `writeActions[]`, but nothing linked an `open_position` from run N to its `close_position` 6 runs later. The agent had no idea which entry justifications historically worked — quality-matrix-manager's run log showed 7 runs of "thesis-only" reflection with zero concrete PnL feedback. New learning loop, fully file-backed:
  - **Run-log schema extension at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs)** — `runSummary.closedPositions[]` is now populated by `recordClosedPosition({vault, assetId, isLong, ticker, closedReason, closeJustification, realizedPnlUsdc, realizedPnlPctOfCollateral})` (line ~3103). The helper calls `findMatchingOpen(...)` to walk the current run's `writeActions[]` first and then `recentRuns[]` (newest-first) to find the matching `open_position` by `(vault, assetId, isLong)`, then `buildClosedPositionEntry(...)` emits `{ticker, side, entryJustification, closeJustification, entryRunId, entryAt, closedAt, holdHours, realizedPnlUsdc, realizedPnlPctOfCollateral, closedReason}`. Duplicate closes on the same `(vault, assetId, isLong)` within a single run are collapsed into the first entry so the log stays clean. Persisted in the run-log payload alongside `writeActions` and `riskOfficerVerdicts`.
  - **Realized PnL is captured opportunistically** because `close_position` itself doesn't return realized PnL. For `autoRebalanceClose` (line ~2854) the runner already has the open-positions roster with per-leg `unrealisedPnlUsdc` / `unrealisedPnlPctOfCollateral` from the GMX read; for LLM-driven `close_position` calls (line ~3021) the runner reads the same fields from `policyRuntime.lastOpenPositionsRoster`, which is populated whenever the LLM calls `list_open_positions` or `get_perp_capital_snapshot`. When the roster isn't fresh, the entry still records the open match + justification — only `realizedPnl*` is left null.
  - **`buildLessonsBlock({runs})` helper at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) line ~714** scans the last 30 days of `recentRuns`, flattens every `closedPositions[]` array, picks the top-3 winners + top-3 losers by `realizedPnlPctOfCollateral`, and emits a compact 6-line summary (`Wins: GSR.V long +14% (3 days, "Exceptional GT=754"); ...` / `Losses: PWM.V long -7% ...`). Injected at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) line ~1714 right after "Recent Run History" in the system prompt assembly — exactly where the plan landed.
  - **`agents/skills/lessons.md`** documents the `closedPositions[]` schema, how `realizedPnl*` is captured, and how the `## Lessons` block should be interpreted. Both [agents/mining-manager.md](agents/mining-manager.md) and [agents/quality-matrix-manager.md](agents/quality-matrix-manager.md) declare the skill in frontmatter so it shows up in the per-agent skills list.
  - **Tests:** new [scripts/agent-runner-lessons.test.mjs](scripts/agent-runner-lessons.test.mjs) covers `findMatchingOpen` (current-run match, recent-runs match, no-match, mismatched `isLong`), `buildClosedPositionEntry` (with + without realized PnL, with + without matching open), and `buildLessonsBlock` (no closures → empty string, single win, full 6-line top-3 / bottom-3, ties broken by closedAt, custom `windowMs`).

- [2026-05-23 06:08 UTC+05:30] **Shared news cache + per-vault churn guard — agents stop re-fetching the same Yahoo headlines on every tick and stop re-opening tickers the runner just rotated out (Quick Win 4 from [trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md](.cursor/plans/trading_bot_quick_wins_a90b8b8a.plan.md)).** Background: mining-manager and quality-matrix-manager run back-to-back in the same hourly cron tick and both fetch `yfinance_news` on overlapping symbols, paying full tool latency twice. Worse, when `autoExitMode: rank_swap+pnl_band` rotated a ticker out (e.g. dropped from quality top-N), nothing stopped the agent from re-opening it 60 minutes later — straight rank-swap thrash, two round-trip GMX fees per swap. New cross-agent blackboard:
  - **New shared-memory module at [apps/shared/agent-shared-memory.mjs](apps/shared/agent-shared-memory.mjs)** exporting `getCachedNews({symbol, ttlMs})`, `writeNewsCache({symbol, agentName, headlines})`, `readRecentlyClosed({vault})`, `checkChurnGuard({vault, assetId, windowMs})`, `recordRecentlyClosed({vault, assetId, isLong, ticker, reason})`, plus constants `NEWS_CACHE_TTL_MS_DEFAULT = 30 min` and `CHURN_GUARD_WINDOW_MS = 4 hours`. All file IO is atomic (write-temp-then-rename) and the on-disk layout deliberately AVOIDS shared filenames: every agent writes its own `news-cache.<agent>.json` and every vault writes its own `recently-closed.<vault>.json` under `agents/memory/shared/`, so concurrent CI artifact uploads of the same directory can never clobber each other (the same artifact-merge corruption class that hit the per-agent memory directories in the 2026-05-23 02:19 entry below). Reads union across every per-agent cache file and pick the freshest entry per symbol, so caching benefits compose across agents.
  - **`yfinance_news` consults the cache first** at [apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js](apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js) line ~210 (cache lookup) and ~266 (cache write on miss). The response shape gains three transparency fields: `_cacheHit: boolean`, `_cacheFetchedAt: ISO`, and `_cacheSourceAgent: string` (which agent originally fetched the cached entry) so an operator scanning the run log can see at a glance whether a tool latency spike came from a real Yahoo fetch or a cache miss. Env knobs `AGENT_NEWS_CACHE_DISABLED=1` (bypass entirely) and `AGENT_NEWS_CACHE_TTL_MS=<ms>` (override the default 30 min). `agents/mcp-servers.json` threads `AGENT_NAME`, `AGENT_NEWS_CACHE_TTL_MS`, and `AGENT_NEWS_CACHE_DISABLED` through `envPassthrough` so MCP children get the right identity.
  - **`plan_open_position` consults the churn guard** at [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) lines ~977-1013 — if the same `(vault, assetId)` was closed by ANY actor (auto-exit `rank_swap`, `pnl_band:above_take_profit`, `pnl_band:below_stop_loss`, or `llm_judged`) inside the 4-hour cooldown window, it returns `{success: false, error_code: "CHURN_GUARD_COOLDOWN", cooldownEndsAt, lastCloseReason, lastClosedAt, recovery_hint}` so the LLM skips and waits. Override path: pass `bypassChurnGuard: true` together with `bypassReason: "<short audit string>"` — both are persisted into `runSummary.writeActions` for audit. Env knobs `AGENT_CHURN_GUARD_DISABLED=1` / `AGENT_CHURN_GUARD_WINDOW_MS=<ms>` for testing. [agents/skills/vault-manager.md](agents/skills/vault-manager.md) `plan_open_position` row documents both the error code and the bypass path.
  - **Runner records every close into the guard** via `recordCloseInChurnGuard({vault, assetId, isLong, ticker, reason})` at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) — called from both `autoRebalanceClose` (rank-swap + pnl-band exits) and `executeToolCall` (LLM-driven `close_position`), so every close path feeds the guard. The runner also sets `process.env.AGENT_NAME = config.name` before spawning MCPs so news cache writes are tagged with the right agent.
  - **CI persists the shared dir** — [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml) artifact-upload paths now include `agents/memory/shared/`, and the pre-extract cleanup step (`rm -rf agents/memory/*/`) was tightened to explicitly preserve `agents/memory/shared/` so the per-agent memory-dir cleanup added in commit `14d5dc4` no longer wipes shared cache between cron ticks.
  - **Docs + tests:** [agents/memory/shared/README.md](agents/memory/shared/README.md) describes the layout, per-file ownership, and CI lifecycle so operators know the directory is bot-managed. New [apps/shared/agent-shared-memory.test.mjs](apps/shared/agent-shared-memory.test.mjs) covers atomic writes, TTL expiry, churn-guard window math, the freshest-entry-wins union read across multiple per-agent files, and the `bypassChurnGuard` / `recordRecentlyClosed` round-trip.

- [2026-05-23 05:33 UTC+05:30] **Asset price charts now have a Source selector (On-chain / Yahoo Finance / Both) on both the basket detail mini-chart grid and the `/prices/[assetId]` full-page chart.** Background: until now the asset-price charts at [apps/web/src/components/baskets/asset-price-panel.tsx](apps/web/src/components/baskets/asset-price-panel.tsx) and [apps/web/src/app/prices/[assetId]/page.tsx](apps/web/src/app/prices/[assetId]/page.tsx) only plotted the on-chain oracle history indexed by Envio (`OraclePriceUpdate` entities from `PriceUpdated` events). For assets that update infrequently — or before the keeper runs at all on a fresh deployment — the chart could show "Not enough data" while the underlying market clearly had moved. Yahoo Finance was already integrated for spot quotes (`/api/yahoo-finance/quote`) and admin asset wiring, plus a CLI seeder in [scripts/fetch-historical-prices.js](scripts/fetch-historical-prices.js), but never surfaced as a chart series. New stack:
  - **`/api/yahoo-finance/history` route** at [apps/web/src/app/api/yahoo-finance/history/route.ts](apps/web/src/app/api/yahoo-finance/history/route.ts) wraps `yahoo-finance2`'s `chart()` API with a window→interval map (`24H`→`5m`, `7D`→`1h`, `30D`→`1d`), reuses the same `currency → USD` FX cache pattern as the existing quote route (with `GBp → GBP` divide-by-100 handling), and adds a 60 s in-memory response cache keyed by `symbol+window` to limit Yahoo request volume. Returns `{ symbol, resolvedSymbol, window, interval, currency, fxRate, points: [{ timestamp, priceUsd }] }`.
  - **`useYahooPriceHistory` hook** at [apps/web/src/hooks/useYahooPriceHistory.ts](apps/web/src/hooks/useYahooPriceHistory.ts) is a React Query wrapper that mirrors the shape of `useOraclePriceHistory` so charts can consume either source from the same point type. Disabled when no Yahoo symbol is provided so it can be wired into UI for assets without a usable ticker without firing requests.
  - **Shared symbol map** moved into [apps/shared/yahoo-symbol-map.mjs](apps/shared/yahoo-symbol-map.mjs) with `YAHOO_SYMBOL_MAP` (XAU→GC=F, XAG→SI=F) and an `oracleSymbolToYahooSymbol` helper that drops 0x-prefixed address-style placeholders. Re-exported from [apps/web/src/lib/yahoo-finance.ts](apps/web/src/lib/yahoo-finance.ts) for the components and used by [scripts/fetch-historical-prices.js](scripts/fetch-historical-prices.js) so the script and the new route stay in lockstep.

- [2026-05-23 05:32 UTC+05:30] **`plan_open_position` MCP sizing helper in [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) that converts target leverage + free vault collateral into the exact raw `size` and `collateral` integers safe to pass straight into `open_position`.** Background: quality-matrix-manager run `2026-05-22T23:53:07Z` (vault `0xbd7ea7e23ae07f0dd65e0738babf8864fdd741f3`) burned 8 of 23 turns retrying `open_position` on Giant Mining Corp. — the first two attempts reverted with `Vault: maxLeverage exceeded` (selector `0x08c379a0` against the GMX-fork check at [src/gmx/core/VaultUtils.sol#L161](src/gmx/core/VaultUtils.sol) — `remainingCollateral × maxLeverage < size × 10_000` where `maxLeverage = 500_000` = 50x), and the next six reverted with `Vault: liquidation fees exceed collateral` ([src/gmx/core/VaultUtils.sol#L156](src/gmx/core/VaultUtils.sol) — `remainingCollateral < marginFees + liquidationFeeUsd` with `liquidationFeeUsd = 5e30` = $5, set via the `gmxVault.initialize(routerAddr, usdgAddr, pfAddr, 5e30, 600, 600)` call at [script/Deploy.s.sol#L133](script/Deploy.s.sol)). Direct cause: the LLM started at `$1,000` notional on `$10` collateral (100x leverage — the agent had only ~$15 free after the two existing legs locked $1,980 of the $1,990 perp-allocated capital) and then bisected by halving BOTH `size` and `collateral` together, so every retry kept the same 100:1 ratio and either hit the 50x cap or pushed collateral below the $5 liquidation-fee floor. The pre-flight at [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js#L1448](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) only catches `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` (`collateral > available`), not leverage or the liquidation-fee floor, and the agent prompt at [agents/quality-matrix-manager.md](agents/quality-matrix-manager.md) line 118 just said *"collateral must be at least 10% of position size"* without any raw-integer recipe, `liquidationFeeUsd` floor, or `get_perp_capital_snapshot` precheck. New tool fix:
  - **`plan_open_position({ vault, assetId, isLong, targetLeverage?, numNewSlots?, maxCollateralUsdcRaw? })`** read-only handler inserted at [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) immediately after `get_perp_capital_snapshot` (same `readVaultAccountingState` + `buildOpenPositionsRoster` plumbing). Computes `perSlot = accounting.available / numNewSlots`, optionally clamps by `maxCollateralUsdcRaw`, then derives `sizeRaw = collateralRaw * 1e24 * round(targetLeverage * 100) / 100` (two-digit leverage precision so `10.5x` round-trips cleanly). Returns `{ vault, assetId, isLong, leverage, size, collateral, collateral_usdc, notionalUsd, availableCollateral, availableCollateral_usdc, numNewSlots, chainCaps:{maxLeverage:50, liquidationFeeUsd:"5", minCollateralPerSlot}, warnings, nextSteps:[{ tool:"open_position", params_hint:{ vault, assetId, isLong, size, collateral } }] }`. Hard caps enforced inline (matching the on-chain reverts): `targetLeverage ∈ (0, 50]` (`INVALID_ARGUMENT` otherwise), and `collateralRaw ≥ 10_000_000` raw USDC = $10 (2x the on-chain $5 `liquidationFeeUsd` buffer). When per-slot collateral falls below the floor, returns structured `error_code: "INSUFFICIENT_FREE_COLLATERAL_FOR_LIQ_FEE_BUFFER"` with the open-position roster embedded (same shape as the `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL` payload at line 1475) and a `recovery_hint` directing the LLM to either narrow `numNewSlots`, drop `maxCollateralUsdcRaw`, or close the worst-PnL leg via `close_position` before retrying. Soft warnings on the success path flag `targetLeverage > 20x` and collateral within 2x of the floor.
  - **`MIN_COLLATERAL_USDC_RAW = 10_000_000n` / `CHAIN_MAX_LEVERAGE = 50` / `USDC_TO_GMX_USD_SCALE = 10n ** 24n` / `LIQUIDATION_FEE_USD = "5"`** constants hoisted into the env-config block at the top of `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js` (just after `PNL_BAND_TP_PCT` / `PNL_BAND_SL_PCT`) with a header comment citing the GMX `VaultUtils.validateLiquidation` math and the `script/Deploy.s.sol` initialiser args so the source-of-truth is documented alongside the constants.
  - **`open_position` tool description tightened** at `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js` line ~1394 to surface the two on-chain caps (`maxLeverage 50x`, `~$10 min collateral`) inline and add a *"RECOMMENDED: call `plan_open_position` first"* line that names both revert strings the helper now prevents, so even an agent that hasn't been re-prompted yet will see the safer path in the tool list.

- [2026-05-23 05:30 UTC+05:30] **Collateral-aware open positions + deterministic rank-swap auto-exit in the vault-manager MCP and agent runner.** Background: mining-manager run `2026-05-22T23:23:16` (vault `0x4dcd435461e27f8bfb580d216b8d69490023a0ba`) burned turns 8–11 on `open_position` calls that the on-chain `require(collateral <= available)` was always going to revert — the vault had `depositedCapital=$2,715.35` with `collateralLocked=$2,700`, so only $15 was free but the LLM proposed 3 longs at $500 collateral each. The pre-flight at [apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js#L1320](apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js) already short-circuited with `INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL`, but the recovery hint just pointed the LLM at `list_open_positions + get_vault_pnl` without telling it which leg to close. The deterministic auto-rebalance at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs#L2321](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) only closed legs that dropped out of the ML top-N — all 4 open legs were still in the top-12 (just ranked #5-#8 against fresh #1-#3 picks), so it closed nothing and the LLM was stuck. Fix is in three layers:
  - **`apps/mcps/vault-manager/position-pnl.mjs`** (new): pure `computePositionPnl({isLong, size, averagePrice, currentOraclePrice, collateralUsdc, takeProfitPct, stopLossPct})` helper that derives `unrealisedPnlUsdc`, `unrealisedPnlPctOfCollateral`, and `pnlBandOutcome ∈ {"within","above_take_profit","below_stop_loss","unknown"}` from GMX 1e30 size + oracle 1e8 prices using BigInt math (`pnl_e30 = size_e30 * priceDelta_e8 / avgPrice_e8`, then `/1e24` to USDC 6-dec). Defaults `PNL_BAND_DEFAULTS = {takeProfitPct: 0.08, stopLossPct: 0.06}` match the mining-manager prompt's `[-6%, +8%]` band, override via env `MCP_PNL_BAND_TP_PCT` / `MCP_PNL_BAND_SL_PCT`. Unit-tested in `apps/mcps/vault-manager/list-positions-pnl.test.mjs` (9 cases: long up, long down, short flip, within-band, zero/null avgPrice, null currentOraclePrice, zero collateral, custom thresholds, default constants).
  - **`apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js`**: shared `buildOpenPositionsRoster(vault, d)` helper extracted from `list_open_positions` and reused by both the `open_position` INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL embed and the new `get_perp_capital_snapshot` tool. `list_open_positions` now emits the per-leg PnL fields on every position. New tool `get_perp_capital_snapshot({vault})` is a one-shot read returning `{idleUsdc, perpAllocated, accounting:{depositedCapital, collateralLocked, availableCollateral, ...}, pnlBand:{takeProfitPct, stopLossPct}, openPositions[]}` — the description nudges the LLM to call it before every `open_position` so collateral sizing never blows the on-chain check. Tool-shape regressions guarded by `apps/mcps/vault-manager/open-position-roster.test.mjs`.
  - **`scripts/agent-runner.mjs`**: new `autoExitMode` policy knob in `parseAgentPolicy` accepting `none | rank_swap | pnl_band | rank_swap+pnl_band` (`+` and `,` both allowed, normalised to sorted `+`-join). `rank_swap` requires `rebalanceMode: track_top_n`. Two new pure decision helpers next to `computeAutoRebalanceClosures`: `computePnlBandClosures({policy, positions})` closes any long leg whose `pnlBandOutcome` is `above_take_profit` / `below_stop_loss`; `computeRankSwapClosures({policy, positions, rankedPicks, availableCollateralUsdc, minSlotCollateralUsdc})` closes the lowest-ranked-then-worst-PnL long legs to make room when higher-ranked top-N picks are blocked by locked capital (`slotsToFree = max(0, picksWanted.length - floor(available / minSlot))`). Both respect `entryDirection` (never rotates shorts in `long_short`, no-op in `short_only`). Wired into `enforceAutoRebalance` as a 3-pass: dropouts → pnl_band → re-read `get_perp_capital_snapshot` → rank_swap. Per-slot collateral is derived from `depositedCapital / maxTrackedAssets` (the equal-weight steady-state target). Both helpers exported via `__agentRunnerInternals` and covered by 7 + 3 cases in `scripts/agent-runner-policy.test.mjs`.

  Together: the LLM now sees a roster with per-leg PnL in the INSUFFICIENT_COLLATERAL error and via `get_perp_capital_snapshot`, AND the runner deterministically rotates the worst-rank existing long out before the LLM turn whenever a higher-ranked top-N pick is blocked, so the loop above can't repeat under `autoExitMode: rank_swap+pnl_band`.

### Changed

- [2026-05-23 05:32 UTC+05:30] **`quality-matrix-manager` agent ([agents/quality-matrix-manager.md](agents/quality-matrix-manager.md)) opts into `autoExitMode: rank_swap+pnl_band` and now sizes every new open via `plan_open_position`, closing the gap with `mining-manager`.** Frontmatter (line 34) adds `autoExitMode: rank_swap+pnl_band` so the runner's pre-LLM auto-rebalance pass at [scripts/agent-runner.mjs#L2537](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) `enforceAutoRebalance` now closes (a) dropouts from the quality top-N, (b) `pnlBandOutcome` breaches outside `[-6%, +8%]`, and (c) the lowest-ranked existing long(s) when higher-ranked top-N picks are blocked by locked capital. Previously the pass logged `[AUTO-REBALANCE] All N open positions remain in Quality top-12 and within PnL band; checking rank-swap.` and exited without rotating — exactly what happened on the failing `2026-05-22T23:53:07Z` run, leaving the LLM ~$15 of free collateral while it tried to fund a fresh long. Workflow step 10 picks up a new "Sizing precheck" bullet directing the LLM to call `get_perp_capital_snapshot({ vault })` once and then `plan_open_position` for every open, passing `targetLeverage: 10` for longs / `5` (or lower) for shorts, with `numNewSlots` = count of new opens this turn. Long-lane wording switches from *"split `availableForPerp` evenly"* (idle vault USDC, wrong denominator post-allocation) to *"split `accounting.availableCollateral` evenly across new entrants by passing `numNewSlots` to `plan_open_position`"*. Short-lane wording adds two concrete recipes for the `≤ 50% of long sizing` rule (`targetLeverage: 5` OR `maxCollateralUsdcRaw` = half the long per-slot collateral). Closes paragraph is rewritten verbatim from the mining-manager pattern (the three deterministic cases the runner now handles before the LLM turn). Key Rules grows three new bullets replacing the single *"collateral must be at least 10% of position size"* line: (1) always derive `size`/`collateral` from `plan_open_position` — never hand-roll 1e30 math; (2) $10 raw (`10000000`) minimum collateral per leg, citing the on-chain `liquidationFeeUsd` revert; (3) hard 50x chain cap with explicit note that halving both `size` and `collateral` together does NOT reduce leverage (the exact mistake the LLM made 8 times in the failing run). The auto-rebalance Key Rule is also rewritten to enumerate the three deterministic cases. User Prompt at the bottom of the file now names `plan_open_position` for both long and short sizing. `mining-manager` and `vault-manager` are untouched — both still work; they can opt into `plan_open_position` later but aren't required to. [agents/skills/vault-manager.md](agents/skills/vault-manager.md) gains `plan_open_position`, `get_perp_capital_snapshot`, and `list_open_positions` rows in the Read Tools table plus a new "Safe perp sizing" subsection under the units cheat sheet that documents the three-step precheck → plan → open flow.

Step 9 picks up a new "Sizing precheck" bullet directing the LLM to call `get_perp_capital_snapshot` before every open, and the "Closes" bullet enumerates the three deterministic cases the runner now handles (dropouts, PnL band, rank-swap). The short-side rules are unchanged: rank-swap and pnl-band never touch shorts in `long_short`, so the LLM still owns all short exits and any judgement-driven close needing news context. ### Removed

- [2026-05-23 01:48 UTC+05:30] **Dropped the "Show latest run summary" toggle from the AI Operator card on the basket detail page (`apps/web/src/app/baskets/[address]/page.tsx`).** The collapsible `<pre>` block that rendered `agentMeta.latestRun.summary` (and the `summaryOpen` `useState` that backed it) was redundant once the "Show all decisions" panel started rendering the latest run with a `Latest` pill, structured per-action params, and justifications — the free-text summary was just a less-structured restatement of the same content. The card now goes Thesis → All decisions with no intermediate toggle.

### Fixed

- [2026-05-23 05:05 UTC+05:30] **Vault-agent CI `commit-results` job no longer silently deletes tracked agent memory from `main` and no longer dead-locks on a modify/delete rebase conflict on the next tick.** Root cause was the pipeline in [.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml](.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml) `commit-results`: `rm -rf agents/memory/*/` → `actions/download-artifact@v4` → `git add agents/memory/ apps/web/public/agent-metadata/`. `git add <dir>` stages **deletions** for tracked files no longer present on disk, so whenever an agent's upload artifact happened to contain nothing under `agents/memory/<agent>/` (early crash before the memory dir was created, missing subtree), the post-`rm` working tree was missing those files and the commit silently wiped them from `main`. That is exactly what produced commit `b7033c1 memory(agent): refresh memory only` — its only diff was deletions of `agents/memory/quality-matrix-manager/{state.json, run-log.sepolia.jsonl}`, and the "refresh memory only" subject is the `agentNames.length === 0` fallback in [scripts/build-agent-commit-message.mjs](scripts/build-agent-commit-message.mjs) line 340 (deleted-file paths read back as `null` and so populate neither `metadataByAgent` nor `runLogByAgent`). The very next tick's `quality-matrix-manager` ran and appended a run-log entry via the failure-path `appendRunLog` in [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) line 3000, producing local commit `b2239fb` that **modified** the just-deleted files; `git pull --rebase` then hit a modify/delete tree conflict and the job exited 1, dropping that run's memory on the floor. Three-part fix in the workflow:
  - **`git add --ignore-removal agents/memory/ apps/web/public/agent-metadata/`** instead of plain `git add`. `--ignore-removal` is the explicit opposite of `-A` (see `git add --help`): only additions and modifications get staged, deletions of tracked files are skipped. The pre-extract `rm -rf` belt-and-braces step still protects against the original artifact-merge corruption fixed in commit `14d5dc4`, but its "missing files" side effect can no longer leak into commits. The only way to delete an agent's memory from `main` is now an explicit human commit.
  - **`git pull --rebase --strategy-option=theirs`** instead of bare `git pull --rebase`. In rebase terms "theirs" = the commits being replayed (the local agent's freshly produced state), so any residual content conflict on agent-memory files auto-resolves in favor of the agent's update instead of deadlocking the push. Safe because every file under `agents/memory/<agent>/` is write-once-per-run by `agent-runner.mjs` and never hand-edited.
  - **Restored `agents/memory/quality-matrix-manager/{state.json, run-log.sepolia.jsonl}`** from the last good commit `7b2927f` (`git checkout 7b2927f -- ...`). The state pins vault `0xbd7ea7e23ae07f0dd65e0738babf8864fdd741f3`, deployment fingerprint `sha256:5e53609e…`, and the current thesis; the run-log carries the recent-runs context the agent uses to prompt itself. The matching public metadata file `apps/web/public/agent-metadata/0xbd7ea7…1f3.json` was never touched by `b7033c1` so the UI side is already intact.

- [2026-05-23 04:43 UTC+05:30] **Mining-manager (and the matching quality-matrix-manager) no longer loops on `set_vault_assets` and `wire_asset` until `maxTurns` when the LLM calls `get_oracle_assets({compact:true})`.** On Sepolia run `2026-05-22T22:58:50` (vault `0x4dcd435461e27f8bfb580d216b8d69490023a0ba`), turn 2 picked the compact shape (which intentionally omits `assets[]` and returns only `{count, summary:{symbols, activeSymbols, symbolToAssetId}}` — see `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js` lines 583-632). The three eligibility helpers in [scripts/agent-runner.mjs](scripts/agent-runner.mjs) (`getEligibleMomentumVolumeAssets`, `getEligibleQualityScoreAssets`, `getEligibleMlScoreAssets`) all short-circuited to `[]` because their guard checked `!Array.isArray(oracleAssets?.assets)`, so `eligibleAssets.length` stayed at 0 for the whole run; the `needsRoll` enforcement branch (`opensExecuted === 0 && eligibleAssets.length === 0 && actionablePicks.length > 0`) then fired on every turn and the directive at lines 2660-2667 — which built `oracleSymbolSet` from the same missing `latestOracleAssets.assets` — listed all 12 top-N picks as "not yet wired". The LLM retried `wire_asset` for `0R2O.L` (Atlas's LSE depository line for Freeport-McMoRan, which Yahoo cannot resolve → permanent `INVALID_SYMBOL_POLICY`) and `VGZ.TO` (already wired at `0xc046404b…d515a6b2de` → permanent `ALREADY_WIRED`) seven times, fired 7 redundant `set_vault_assets` transactions (`0xf46f…0539d`, `0x51d4…20d01`, `0x3e36…07a6f`, `0x1bb3…9b302`, `0x30a6…67fc`, `0xaad4…d5a68`, `0xe48f…63fa8`), opened 0 new positions, and ran out the clock at turn 40. Three-part fix in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs`:
  - **`normalizeOracleAssets(oracleAssets)` helper** between `computeAutoAllocationAmount` and `getEligibleMomentumVolumeAssets`. Returns `oracleAssets.assets` when present, otherwise projects `summary.symbolToAssetId` into a minimal `[{ symbol, assetId }]` array (the only two fields the eligibility helpers consume). All three `getEligible*Assets` helpers and the `needsRoll` directive's `oracleSymbolSet` builder now go through this helper, so `eligibleAssets` reflects reality whether the LLM called `get_oracle_assets()` or `get_oracle_assets({compact:true})`. Exported via `__agentRunnerInternals` for tests.
  - **No-op guard on `set_vault_assets`** inside `executeToolCall` (right after the existing `get_all_vaults` skip block). Before the MCP call fires, the runner compares `args.assetIds` against `policyRuntime.latestVaultState.assets` (lowercased set equality); if they match, it skips the cast send entirely and returns a synthetic `{success:true, skipped:"NO_CHANGE", ...}` tool result so the LLM moves on without a turn-burning revise loop. Records the skip under `runSummary.writeActions` with `skipped:true, skipReason:"NO_CHANGE"`. Same shape as the existing `allocate_to_perp amount=0` guard.
  - **Per-run wire-failure blacklist** under new `policyRuntime.persistentWireFailures: Set<string>`. When `wire_asset` returns `error_code:"INVALID_SYMBOL_POLICY"`, the runner logs `[POLICY] Blacklisted <symbol> for this run` and adds the upper-cased symbol to the set. The `needsRoll` directive then strips blacklisted symbols from `unwiredPicks` and appends a `Skip these symbols entirely for this run (wire_asset already failed with INVALID_SYMBOL_POLICY): <list>.` note, so the LLM stops re-proposing structurally-impossible wires like `0R2O.L` on every enforcement round.

  Together these three changes break the loop at three distinct layers (eligibility intersection, redundant on-chain write, repeated impossible wire). The follow-up data fix for Atlas surfacing `0R2O.L` itself still lives in the upstream `atlas-prototype` repo (see the 2026-05-23 04:23 entry — Yahoo pre-validation pre-existed but only filters new picks; `0R2O.L` is grandfathered from a pre-validation snapshot).

- [2026-05-23 04:23 UTC+05:30] **Mining-manager + quality-matrix-manager agents recover from the five distinct failure modes that were dominating CI runs (CRML/VGZ.TO retry storms, 0R2O.L symbol policy rejections, mining-manager `Insufficient capital for collateral` reverts, the 28-byte hallucinated vault on the quality-matrix `open_position` call, and the silent tail-truncation of `get_oracle_assets`).** Decoded each on-chain: `wire_asset(CRML)` was reverting with selector `0x8a30b646` = `MappingAlreadyExists(bytes32)` from [src/perp/PriceSync.sol#L50](src/perp/PriceSync.sol) because `wire_asset` was not idempotent and re-attempted to wire CRML even though it was already at `OracleAdapter.assetList[15]` (active=true, verified via `cast call OracleAdapter.getAssetConfig(keccak256("CRML"))` against Sepolia); the mining-manager vault `0x4dcd435461e27f8bfb580d216b8d69490023a0ba` had `depositedCapital=$2715.36 / collateralLocked=$2700` leaving ~$15 free while the LLM kept proposing $1700 collateral opens; the quality-matrix-manager LLM emitted vault `0xbd7ea7e23ae07f0dd65b2bf6ecc95018c610da029ccb697f17b69b2` (28 bytes — prefix of the real vault concatenated with the suffix of GRSL.V's assetId) and `cast send` rejected it with an opaque parser error; the full `get_oracle_assets` payload (~6.9 KB at 26 assets) sat above the 6000-char `AGENT_MAX_TOOL_RESPONSE` budget so tail entries were silently truncated before reaching the LLM; and `0R2O.L` was Atlas's LSE depository line for Freeport-McMoRan (NYSE `FCX`), which Yahoo doesn't quote — the `atlas-ml` MCP `EXCHANGE_SUFFIX_MAP` mapped `LSE` → `.L` and surfaced an unresolvable symbol every run. Seven-layer fix landed in this edit:
