  - **Web app:** `apps/web/src/components/baskets/multi-chain-deposit-drawer.tsx` now reads the basket name via `useReadContract({ functionName: "name", chainId: referenceChainId })`, feeds it into the new hook, and (a) builds `vaultMappings` only from chains with a non-null twin address, (b) filters `routingWeights` to chains that appear in `vaultMappings` before calling `computeDepositSplits` (so a Sepolia-only basket collapses to 100% Sepolia even when the keeper-posted table includes Fuji), (c) renders a new `data-testid="multi-chain-missing-twin-note"` warning panel listing every chain that was dropped, (d) widens the loading-state gate from `weightsLoading` to `weightsLoading || nameLoading || vaultMatchesLoading` so the "no routing configuration" empty-state can't briefly flash during resolution, and (e) the empty-state copy now branches between "no routing weights configured", "basket is single-chain only", and "basket is not deployed on any configured chain" so the user gets a specific error rather than a generic fallback.
  - **End-to-end validation (Phase E test deposits, deployer wallet):** Sepolia ML Picks $20 deposit → `0xee93c830…64107a5b` (USDC $2,000 → $2,020, shares 1,990 → 2,009.9, 50bps fee applied correctly). Fuji Bootstrap $20 deposit → `0xf3e64461…8567cb3866` — FIRST EVER user shares on this vault (totalSupply 0 → 20). Fuji ML Picks twin $20 deposit (after the `setAssets` fix above) → `0x59fbadc9…34c7696e` (USDC $1,000 → $1,020, shares 0 → 19.9). All three confirmed `status: 1 (success)` on their respective explorers.
  - **Tests:** new `apps/web/src/hooks/useVaultAddressByName.test.ts` (4 tests) covers the trimmed-lowercase query key, the `enabled` gating when `vaultName` or `referenceVaultAddress` is missing, and the basic round-trip of resolved matches through `useQuery`. Full web suite still green (`npx vitest run`: 87 / 87 across 23 files pre-change, 91 / 91 across 24 files post-change); `npx tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **Docs:** `docs/CROSS_CHAIN_COORDINATION.md` "Frontend Deposit Splitting" section now documents the per-chain twin basket requirement and the recommended deployment pattern (`createBasket` + `setAssets` + `setStateRelay` + `topUpReserve`). `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md` updated with rows for the two new Fuji twin baskets (agent-owned, `read`/`deploy`/`update-config` allowed actions).
  - **Out of scope (called out, not changed):** Redemption CCIP path (`RedemptionReceiver.setTrustedSender` still not wired — no hub-side redemption sender contract exists). Per-chain unified share token (each twin has its own share ERC20; cross-chain deposits produce separate share balances). Asset wiring on the Fuji twin baskets (they hold idle reserve but have no real oracle-backed asset list — agents continue to operate against the Sepolia originals only; the Fuji twins exist purely for the deposit-splitting demo).

### Changed

- [2026-05-22 23:45 UTC+05:30] **Privy embedded-wallet transactions now run headlessly with an in-app stepper + a floating transaction dock — no more popup interrupting deposits, approvals, or redeems.** Sets `embeddedWallets.showWalletUIs: false` globally in `apps/web/src/config/privy.ts` (per the [Privy whitelabel](https://docs.privy.io/wallets/using-wallets/whitelabel) and [manage-wallet-UIs](https://docs.privy.io/recipes/react/manage-wallet-UIs) recipes), and strips the per-call `showWalletUIs` plumbing from `apps/web/src/hooks/useSponsoredTransactionAdapter.ts` (the `SendTxParams.showWalletUIs` field and the `uiOptions: { showWalletUIs }` argument to `useSendTransaction().sendTransaction` are gone — `sendTransaction` is now called with `{ sponsor }` only) as well as the two `showWalletUIs: true` overrides in `apps/web/src/hooks/useParallelChainDeposits.ts` (deposit + approval paths). External wallets are unaffected because `useSponsoredWriteContract` already throws `SponsorshipError` when the active wallet is not the Privy embedded one. The native-sponsor path (Sepolia, Arbitrum Sepolia) is the one this changes; the 4337 smart-wallet path (Fuji) was already headless via `client.sendTransaction`. New file `apps/web/src/providers/TransactionStatusProvider.tsx` introduces a reducer-backed in-memory tx store (`TxRecord { id, kind: "approve"|"deposit"|"redeem"|"multi-chain-deposit"|"admin", label, chainId?, status: "signing"|"submitted"|"confirmed"|"failed", hash?, error?, children?, meta? }`) with action creators `startTx`, `updateTx`, `updateChild`, `markSubmitted`, `completeTx`, `failTx`, `dismissTx`, `clearCompleted`, plus `useTrackedTx({ hash, isPending, isError, error, receipt, kind, label, chainId })` which auto-transitions a record through `signing → submitted → confirmed/failed` from existing wagmi `useWriteContract` + `useWaitForTransactionReceipt` signals. The reducer + initial state are exported (`txStoreReducer`, `initialTxStoreState`, `TxStoreAction`, `TxStoreState`) and covered by 9 new pure unit tests in `apps/web/src/providers/TransactionStatusProvider.test.ts` (start defaults to signing, re-start resets state, signing → submitted, complete-confirmed/failed semantics, dismiss, clear-completed keeps only in-flight, update-child isolates siblings, update-child on parents without children is a no-op). Provider is mounted inside both `PrivyWeb3Provider` and the fallback `Web3Provider` so the dock works in both modes. New component `apps/web/src/components/transactions/transaction-dock.tsx` (mounted once in `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` via dynamic import, `ssr: false`) renders the Rainbow/Sonner-style stacked toast UX described in the Privy/Rainbow inspiration set: up to 3 mini cards in the bottom-right collapsed stack with a `+N more` pill when overflowing, expand-to-full-list view with chain badge, status icon, hash explorer link, dismiss/retry per row, and a `Clear completed` footer; confirmed rows auto-clear after 4s, failed rows persist with an optional retry button that fires `record.meta.onRetry?.()`. Each single-chain write hook in `apps/web/src/hooks/useBasketVault.ts` (`useDeposit`, `useRedeem`, `useApproveUSDC`, plus the admin hooks `useSetMaxPerpAllocation`, `useSetAssets`, `useSetMinReserveBps`, `useTopUpReserve`) now accepts an optional third `TxMeta { label, chainId?, kind? }` argument and stores it in local state so the inner `useTrackedTx` invocation has the correct chain + label at the moment the rising-edge `isPending` effect fires. `apps/web/src/components/baskets/deposit-redeem-panel.tsx` is rewritten to (1) pass concrete labels (`Deposit 250 USDC`, `Redeem 100 shares`, `Approve 250 USDC`) plus `chainId` into the write hooks, (2) drop the `showToast("pending", ...)` and `showToast("success", ...)` calls (the dock + inline stepper own the lifecycle now — error toasts and the `SponsorshipErrorDialog` still trigger via `useContractErrorToast`), and (3) replace the static `Button` with a Uniswap-style inline `InlineTxStepper` component that morphs through four states: `signing` ("Signed silently by your embedded wallet. No popup needed."), `submitted` (spinner + truncated hash + "View" explorer link), `confirmed` (green check + 2.5s linger before reverting to idle; matches the existing `setAmount("")` reset), and `failed` (red border + "Try again" button that re-invokes `handleSubmit`). Hash + explorer URL are derived from `useConfig().chains.find(...).blockExplorers.default.url`. `apps/web/src/components/baskets/multi-chain-deposit-drawer.tsx` is also wired into the global store: on `handleConfirm` it calls `startTx({ kind: "multi-chain-deposit", children: splits.map(...) })` and an effect mirrors every `useParallelChainDeposits` `chainStatuses` update into the parent's children via `updateChild`, rolling up to `completeTx`/`failTx` when all chains finish. This means a user can close the drawer (or navigate away) and still see per-chain progress in the floating dock — the previous drawer-local minimize state died when the Radix dialog unmounted. `apps/web/src/lib/tooltip-copy.ts` gains `headlessSigning` ("Embedded wallet signs silently in the background — no popup. Progress shows here in the card and in the floating transaction dock at the bottom right.") and `transactionDock` entries; `apps/web/README.md` gains a new `## Transaction UX` section describing both surfaces and pointing at the Privy whitelabel docs. The existing `apps/web/src/components/baskets/deposit-redeem-panel.test.ts` still passes unchanged (the `!activeAddress` Connect Wallet path is untouched), the full Vitest suite is green (87 / 87 across 23 files), and `npm run lint` returns 0 errors (34 pre-existing warnings in unrelated files remain). E2E unaffected because `NEXT_PUBLIC_E2E_TEST_MODE=1` already short-circuits `useSponsoredWriteContract` to wagmi's native `useWriteContract` — the headless flip never runs in the E2E mock-wallet path.

- [2026-05-22 23:20 UTC+05:30] **CI vault-agent commit messages now summarise what each agent actually did.** The `commit-results` job in `.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml` previously produced a single hard-coded subject `chore(agent): update agent memory and metadata` for every scheduled / `repository_dispatch` tick, hiding which agent ran, how many on-chain writes landed, and whether the run errored out. New helper `scripts/build-agent-commit-message.mjs` reads the just-staged `apps/web/public/agent-metadata/<vault>.json` files (primary: already-redacted `latestRun.summary` + `recentActions[]` filtered to the latest `runId`) and the per-agent `agents/memory/<agent>/run-log.<network>.jsonl` tails (secondary: `turns`, `errors`, `network`, skipped-write count), groups actions by tool, and emits a Conventional-Commits-shaped message. Subject auto-degrades through four styles (`verbose` -> `headline` -> `compact` -> `ultra`: e.g. `chore(agent): mining-manager: wired 2 + opened 1; quality-matrix-manager: created vault + wired 5` shrinks to `chore(agent): mining-manager (5); quality-matrix-manager (8)`) so every agent's name survives the 72-char cap before any ellipsis truncation kicks in. Body lists per-agent vault address, network, turn count, error count, action groups with tx-confirmation counts (`wire_asset × 2 (1 on-chain)`), up to two justifications per tool, the LLM-authored summary (240-char cap), and a `Run finished:` footer. Failed runs surface as `<agent> FAILED (N errors)` in the subject and an `Errors:` line in the body. Workflow uses `git commit -F` and falls back to the original static message on any script failure (via `::warning::`) so commits never get blocked. New unit-test file `scripts/build-agent-commit-message.test.mjs` (8 tests) covers two-agent rendering, mixed long+short opens, oversized-subject truncation, failed-run rendering, the staged-path classifier, and the `refresh memory only` / default-empty fallbacks. No agent-runner, MCP, or contract changes — every input the script needs (`latestRun.runId` linkage, `txHash`, `justification`, `errors[]`, `turns`, `network`) is already persisted by `publishAgentMetadata` and `appendRunLog` in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs`.

Sepolia (hub) via `CHAIN=sepolia forge script script/Deploy.s.sol:Deploy --slow` — 41 receipts starting at block `10891752`, new addresses recorded in `apps/web/src/config/sepolia-deployment.json`: `BasketFactory 0xa9a83c…145e`, `VaultAccounting 0xc4a01a…86d8` (ships the GMX 1e30 → USDC 6-dec `getVaultPnL` fix AND the `setWirer(BasketFactory, true)` grant baked into `Deploy.s.sol` L199, so the 2026-05-21 17:06 manual `setWirer` follow-up and `script/MigrateVaultAccounting.s.sol` are now obsolete), `OracleAdapter 0x7edb8c…7ea`, `PerpReader 0x8d0F44…55E1`, `PricingEngine 0xcE07DE…cB4C`, `FundingRateManager 0xB10001…fb4A`, `PriceSync 0x886d31…DD12`, `MockUSDC 0x7Cab35…9D67`, `GMXVault 0x3393C6…D696`, `AssetWiring 0x416dE7…D3ce`, `StateRelay 0xA2DcF0…cc7d`. BHP.AX wired in-script as the bootstrap oracle asset; `npm run keeper:once` posted one `StateRelay.updateState` epoch (Sepolia block `10891803`, Fuji block `55607505`). Fuji (spoke) via `CHAIN=fuji forge script script/DeploySpoke.s.sol:DeploySpoke --slow` — 12 receipts starting at block `55607589`, new addresses in `apps/web/src/config/fuji-deployment.json`: `BasketFactory 0xb79721…3c29`, `StateRelay 0xF6fa7d…a515`, `RedemptionReceiver 0x38041d…b5C4`, `bootstrapBasket 0x40a97c…eFd1` ("Fuji Bootstrap Basket"), `MockUSDC 0x09e34E…35D8`. StateRelay keeper on both chains = deployer wallet `0x36716C…9713` (the prior KeeperHub setKeeper config on the old Fuji StateRelay is no longer in the keeper path; KeeperHub was removed per CHANGELOG 2026-05-20). Fuji `RedemptionReceiver.setTrustedSender(...)` is intentionally NOT wired — no Sepolia-side redemption sender contract exists in this stack yet; deferred until that hub-side counterpart is added. `apps/envio/config.yaml` repointed at the new addresses (lowercase, `start_block` aligned to each chain's deploy block), and the stale Fuji `VaultAccounting`/`OracleAdapter` contract entries (which never existed on the spoke and were carried over from an earlier hub-shaped Fuji deploy) are removed. Stale memory wiped: `agents/memory/{mining-manager,vault-manager}/state.json` + `run-log.sepolia.jsonl` + `archive/*` (10 files), `apps/web/public/agent-metadata/0x6723…0c38.json` + `0x2702…b167.json` (the two retired vault-metadata files), and historical broadcast snapshots `broadcast/Deploy.s.sol/11155111/run-1776*.json` (32 files), `broadcast/SeedTestnet.s.sol/11155111/*` (5 files), `broadcast/MigrateVaultAccounting.s.sol/11155111/*` (2 files), `broadcast/DeploySpoke.s.sol/43113/run-1776583569647.json` — only today's `run-latest.json` + matching dated run kept under each script dir. `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md` updated with consolidated Sepolia + Fuji redeploy rows and the legacy "(pending deploy)" / KeeperHub-wallet / VA-migration rows marked SUPERSEDED. Operator follow-ups (NOT performed by agent — require commit + push and Vercel/GH dashboards): (1) commit + push to `main` to trigger Envio Cloud auto-deploy of `indexflow-prototype-2`; resolve the new per-commit URL via `npx envio-cloud deployment endpoint indexflow-prototype-2 <commit-sha>`; (2) rotate `NEXT_PUBLIC_ENVIO_URL` to the new URL in the Vercel project `snx-prototype-web` (production + preview + development) and refresh `apps/web/.env.local`; (3) rotate the GitHub repo secret `ENVIO_URL` to the new URL so the next `.github/workflows/deploy-production.yml` push-worker deploy picks it up — that is the ONLY GitHub secret that needs updating because contract addresses ride in the committed `apps/web/src/config/*-deployment.json` files (not GH secrets) and `KEEPER_PRIVATE_KEY`/`SEPOLIA_RPC_URL`/`FUJI_RPC_URL`/`LLM_API_KEY`/`ATLAS_*` are unchanged.

### Fixed

- [2026-05-22 23:30 UTC+05:30] **Envio HyperIndex re-wired to the app: dashboard / portfolio / baskets / chains pages no longer show zero values when deposits exist on-chain.** Root cause: every handler `eth_call` in `apps/envio/src/utils/contractCalls.ts` was hitting `https://*.rpc.hypersync.xyz`, which is Envio's value-added RPC and requires an `ENVIO_API_TOKEN` Bearer header (verified by curl: `{"error":"Your token is malformed..."}`). The reads were wrapped in silent `try/catch` blocks, so every basket/vault state field that needed an on-chain read silently fell back to the zero defaults — `Basket.sharePrice/usdcBalanceUsdc/tvlBookUsdc/totalSupplyShares/perpAllocatedUsdc/depositFeeBps/redeemFeeBps/minReserveBps/maxPerpAllocation/assetCount` all `"0"`, `VaultStateCurrent.registered = false` even after `vaultRegistered` activity events fired, `BasketExposure` empty, `BasketSnapshot` rows zero, `ChainPoolState` empty (so the `/chains` page was permanently blank). Cumulative counters (`Basket.cumulativeDepositedUsdc`, `UserBasketPosition.shareBalance/netDepositedUsdc`) were correct because those are incremented from event params. The fix:
  - `apps/envio/src/utils/contractCalls.ts` now resolves each chain's RPC URL via env (`SEPOLIA_RPC_URL`, `FUJI_RPC_URL`, `ARBITRUM_SEPOLIA_RPC_URL`, `HUB_RPC_URL`, `SPOKE_RPC_URL`) with public-RPC fallbacks (`https://ethereum-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com`, `https://avalanche-fuji-c-chain-rpc.publicnode.com`, `https://arbitrum-sepolia-rpc.publicnode.com`, plus the dual-Anvil locals). If a `*_RPC_URL` is pointed at a `https://*.rpc.hypersync.xyz` host, the `http()` viem transport is configured with `fetchOptions: { headers: { Authorization: "Bearer ${ENVIO_API_TOKEN}" } }` so the token only attaches where it's needed.
  - Every `readContract` call site swaps its bare `catch {}` for `catch (error) { warnOnce(chainId, "<Contract.fn>", error); }` so future regressions surface as a one-shot `console.warn` per `(chainId, op)` pair instead of silently leaving entity fields at zero. `warnOnce` dedupes via a module-level `Set<string>` keyed on `${chainId}:${op}`.
  - Replaced the per-target fan-out in `apps/web/src/hooks/usePoolReserveRegistry.ts` with a single `useQuery` against the unified Envio endpoint. Envio's GraphQL endpoint already serves rows for all indexed chains keyed by `chainSelector`; the old `useMultiChainSubgraphQuery` fan-out was issuing N identical requests to the same URL and concatenating duplicate rows into the chart. Hook signature changed from `failedTargets: DeploymentTarget[]` (always empty under the unified endpoint) to `isError: boolean`; `apps/web/src/app/chains/page.tsx` now shows a single `"Could not reach the indexer..."` banner when the query errors, and the surrounding test (`apps/web/src/app/chains/page.test.ts`) plus the hook test (`apps/web/src/hooks/usePoolReserveRegistry.test.ts`) were rewritten to mock `useQuery` directly and assert the new shape — 5 chains-related tests still green out of the suite's 78 passing.
  - `apps/web/src/hooks/useHeroProtocolStats.ts` `fetchChainBaskets` was calling `GET_BASKETS_OVERVIEW` with `{ first, skip }` but the query declares `$chainId: Int!`, so the "all chains" hero stats route was throwing a Hasura validation error and silently returning `null`. Added `chainId: chainIdForDeploymentTarget(target)` (matches the pattern in `useMultiChainBaskets`) and imported `chainIdForDeploymentTarget` alongside `DeploymentTarget`.
  - `apps/web/src/lib/subgraph/envio-queries.ts` `ENVIO_GET_BASKET_DETAIL` now declares `$activityFirst: Int!, $activitySkip: Int!` and threads them into `activities(limit: $activityFirst, offset: $activitySkip, ...)` — previously the variables were sent by the call site and silently ignored against a hardcoded `limit: 100`.
  - `apps/envio/.env.example` and `apps/envio/README.md` updated to document the new env vars, the public-RPC defaults, the `ENVIO_API_TOKEN`-only-needed-for-hypersync rule, and the redeploy follow-up required to back-populate the existing deployment at `https://indexer.dev.hyperindex.xyz/dbe3f66/v1/graphql`. The redeploy itself is an operator action (per repo `AGENTS.md` deployment policy) — set `SEPOLIA_RPC_URL`/`FUJI_RPC_URL` (the values already on the repo `.env`) in the Envio Cloud env panel for the `indexflow-prototype-2` indexer, then redeploy so it re-runs handlers from `start_block: 10891752` (Sepolia) / `55607589` (Fuji) and back-populates basket state, vault registration, exposures, snapshots, and chain pool states. No GitHub secret rotation is needed (`NEXT_PUBLIC_ENVIO_URL` only changes if the indexer URL itself changes; the URL stays the same when env vars are updated in place). Why the per-basket detail page LOOKED partly working despite all of this: it reads `useBasketInfo`/`useVaultState`/`useVaultPnL`/`useBasketAssets` and position-tracking directly on-chain via wagmi (`apps/web/src/hooks/usePerpReader.ts` + `apps/web/src/hooks/useBasketVault.ts`), so the metrics strip on a single basket has been showing real numbers all along while the dashboard / portfolio / baskets list / chains page / share-price chart / weekly APY — all four of which source from Envio — were the surfaces stuck at zero.

- [2026-05-22 23:15 UTC+05:30] **Deposit/redeem panel: sticky "processing" state across the approve → mine → allowance-refresh window, and force a deposit-simulation refetch on approval success.** Before this change, `apps/web/src/components/baskets/deposit-redeem-panel.tsx` derived `isProcessing` only from the wagmi `isPending` flag (the wallet-broadcast phase), so the moment a USDC approval tx was broadcast the button snapped back to the `"Approve USDC"` label — looking like nothing had happened — even though the receipt was still mining. Two compounding issues then made the follow-up deposit fail with the same amount keyed in: (a) `useUSDCAllowance` polls on `REFETCH_INTERVAL = 60_000` (`apps/web/src/lib/constants.ts`) and was never manually refetched on approval success, so `needsApproval` stayed `true` for up to 60s after the approval mined; and (b) `useSimulateDeposit` is a `useSimulateContract` query whose inputs (`vault`, `parsedAmount`, `account`) did not change across approve → deposit, so the cached pre-approval `eth_call` revert ("insufficient allowance") kept `blockedBySimulation = true` and `handleSubmit` short-circuited with `showToast("error", "This deposit is likely to fail.")`. The panel now (1) destructures `refetch: refetchAllowance` and `refetch: refetchSimDeposit` off the existing wagmi hooks; (2) tracks an `approvalInFlightAmount: bigint` state set when `approve(...)` is dispatched and cleared in three effects — once `(allowance ?? 0n) >= approvalInFlightAmount`, on `isApproveError || approveReceipt.isError`, and when the user changes the input amount; (3) extends `isProcessing` to `isApprovalConfirming || isDepositConfirming || isRedeemConfirming` where each phase OR's the wagmi `isPending` with `receipt.isLoading`, and `isApprovalConfirming` additionally OR's `approvalInFlightAmount > 0n` so the button stays in the processing state until the on-chain allowance read catches up; (4) calls `refetchAllowance()` and `refetchSimDeposit()` inside the existing `approveReceipt.isSuccess` effect, collapsing the 60s lag and clearing the stale simulation revert before the user can re-submit; (5) early-returns from `handleSubmit` when `isProcessing` to defuse any double-click queued during the confirming window. The button label is also clearer: `apps/web/src/components/ui/icon-helpers.tsx` `getPanelPrimaryActionMeta` now takes an optional `processingAction?: PanelAction | null` hint and emits `"Approving USDC..."`, `"Depositing..."`, or `"Redeeming..."` (each with the existing `Loader2` spinner and the appropriate `accent` / `warning` tone) instead of the generic `"Processing..."`. The panel derives `processingAction` from the three phase flags. Out of scope (called out, not changed): `apps/web/src/components/baskets/admin/reserve-topup-card.tsx` follows the same pattern and has the same latent bug; left untouched per the user's scope ("the deposit panel").

- [2026-05-21 20:02 UTC+05:30] **Sepolia price keeper unblocked: four mining tickers re-seeded after the `wire_asset` price-conversion bug left stale seeds out-of-band vs live Yahoo quotes.** `update-prices.yml` started reverting on every cron tick today with `DeviationTooLarge` (selector `0xbdecbfb7`, 20% guard at 2000 bps) inside `OracleAdapter.submitPrices` — same failure mode as the 2026-04-27 GLEN.L unblock. The CI redaction (`[REDACTED_KEY]` / `***`) was a red herring; the literal revert payload was the contract refusing the new normalised prices. Root cause: today's `quality-matrix-manager` / `mining-manager` `wire_asset` calls seeded the OracleAdapter with prices that were 60-99% off the current live USD quote (likely a unit/FX mismatch in the wiring path — stored values look like raw foreign-currency prices treated as 8-dec USD), so the first regular keeper push tripped the deviation guard and the whole 15-asset `submitPrices` batch reverted on the first offending id. Audit (stored normalised vs `raw × 1e22` from a `DRY_RUN=1` pass of `scripts/update-yahoo-finance-prices.js`) flagged four out-of-band assets; the other 11 were already within the 20% band: AYM.AX stored $4.21 vs live ~$0.0021 (99%), CGNT.V $7.22 vs $0.464 (94%), LGC.V $4.13 vs $1.65 (60%), OOR.V $3.77 vs $0.0399 (99%). Re-seeded each via `OracleAdapter.seedHistoricalPrices(bytes32,uint256[],uint256[])` from the OracleAdapter owner (`0x36716C8C…9713`, same key as the local `.env` `PRIVATE_KEY`); that function is `onlyOwner` and bypasses `_validateDeviation` by design. Sepolia tx hashes: AYM.AX `0xbcabd46fdef229e54c318bf9bc6014970afb99aa3a0e00089d8ef05cd6fcd9ac` (block 10892201, raw 213965), CGNT.V `0x9b1a920a07ce7a529dc90e34e94699a05c19c65addade945cca8fd2e9c1397bf` (block 10892217, raw 46399002), LGC.V `0xa2b5ca5c7803a2bf4fb39e169c467c6164f18b62169138f57a13eb5fb37014f9` (block 10892218, raw 165296443), OOR.V `0xf55c3f0bcfe0f5573a6af9d7a4435b8d406e78bf6429e29da2bfebc4c8372f85` (block 10892219, raw 3987414). Verified end-to-end via `cast call submitPrices(...) --from <owner>` against the full 15-asset payload AND `cast call syncAll()` on `PriceSync 0x886d31…DD12` — both return `0x` (no revert) so the next `cron-tick-pusher` repository_dispatch will succeed. No code changes; the 20% deviation guard stays as the right safety control. Follow-up worth raising as a separate task: the `wire_asset` price-conversion path that seeds `OracleAdapter` from `wireAsset` (`apps/web/src/app/api/yahoo-finance/quote/route.ts` `priceUsd` and downstream callers) — the 2026-04-10 fix only addressed the admin-page wiring; the agent MCP `wire_asset` codepath that runs from `mining-manager` / `quality-matrix-manager` clearly seeds raw foreign-currency or pre-FX values into a USD 8-dec field for at least these four tickers. Defence-in-depth alternative: make `scripts/update-yahoo-finance-prices.js` resilient by either pre-flighting each price via `getPrice` and skipping out-of-band ids, or splitting the batch into per-asset `submitPrice` calls inside try/catch so one bad asset can't take down all 15.

- [2026-05-21 19:05 UTC+05:30] **`wire_asset` accepts bare US tickers even when Yahoo has an unrelated foreign sibling listing.** Root cause: `apps/shared/yahoo-symbol-policy.mjs` `classifySymbolWithSearch` rejected any unsuffixed equity whose Yahoo search family contained at least one `BASE.<suffix>` sibling, regardless of whether that sibling was the same company. Atlas ML returns `{ticker: "CRML", exchange: "NYSE"}` correctly, and `apps/mcps/atlas-ml/index.js` correctly maps NYSE/NASDAQ to an empty Yahoo suffix (Yahoo has no `.US` convention — US listings ARE the bare ticker), but the policy then refused the bare `CRML` because Yahoo also returns `CRML.TA` (Carmel Corp Ltd. on Tel Aviv — a completely unrelated company). Fix: when the requested ticker is unsuffixed AND the exact Yahoo row is on a US venue (`NASDAQ` / `NYSE` / `NMS` / `NGM` / `NCM` / `NYQ` / `BATS` / `ARCA` / `AMEX` / `ASE` / `PCX`, plus any `NASDAQ*` / `NYSE*` prefix match) AND none of the foreign siblings look like the same company by normalized-token Jaccard similarity (`≥ 0.34` after stripping corporate stopwords like `inc`/`corp`/`ltd`/`group`/`holdings`/`ord`/`npv` and `[TICKER]`/parens noise), the policy now returns `reason: "us_listing_with_unrelated_siblings"`, `allowed: true`. Same-company ADR/home pairs (e.g. `BHP` "BHP Group Limited" on NYSE vs `BHP.AX` on ASX) still fall through to `ambiguous_unsuffixed_equity` and require an explicit suffix; missing names default to "same company" so unenriched fixtures stay strict. New tests in `apps/web/src/lib/yahoo-symbol-policy.test.ts` lock in both the CRML allow-case (NASDAQ + Tel Aviv → allowed) and the named-BHP reject-case (NYSE + ASX with identical names → still ambiguous); the original nameless-BHP test is intentionally preserved as the conservative-fallback case. No change to `apps/mcps/atlas-ml/index.js`, `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js`, the `wire_asset` tool signature, or the `INVALID_SYMBOL_POLICY` error code/recovery hint — the policy is the only seam that moves.

- [2026-05-21 18:33 UTC+05:30] **`script/DeploySpoke.s.sol` works under Forge ≥1.5 — drop the inner `vm.startPrank` block in `_maybeBootstrapSpokeBasket`.** Forge 1.5+ rejects `vm.startPrank(...)` while a broadcast is active with `Error: script failed: vm.startPrank: cannot prank for a broadcasted transaction; pass the desired tx.origin into the broadcast cheatcode call`, which made every fresh `CHAIN=fuji forge script DeploySpoke.s.sol` revert at the bootstrap-basket reserve top-up step (right after `MockUSDC.mint(deployer, 100_000_000_000)` succeeded, the next `IMockUSDCBootstrap(ctx.usdc).approve(...)` failed because the surrounding prank call simulated a revert). The fix removes the `vm.startPrank(ctx.deployer)` / `vm.stopPrank()` pair from `_maybeBootstrapSpokeBasket` and inlines the `approve` + `topUpReserve` calls — the active outer `vm.startBroadcast(deployerPrivateKey)` from `run()` already pins `msg.sender` to `ctx.deployer` for any subsequent external call, which is exactly what `basket.topUpReserve` needs because it does `safeTransferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), amount)`. The original comment ("with forge broadcast, prank still targets the deployer") is also reworded to document the Forge ≥1.5 constraint. Validated end-to-end against the 2026-05-21 Fuji clean redeploy — 12 receipts, bootstrap basket `0x40a97c…eFd1` created and immediately topped up with 100,000 mock-USDC reserve as designed.

### Removed

- [2026-05-21 17:27 UTC+05:30] **Retire `vault-manager` agent (vault `Vault Manager Basket`) from CI.** The `.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml` agent matrix no longer includes `vault-manager` on any automated trigger. Specifically: the `vault_entry='{...}'` JSON definition and the `elif [ "${AGENT_INPUT}" = "vault-manager" ]` branch are deleted from the `setup-matrix` `Build matrix from trigger + inputs` step; the schedule / empty-`repository_dispatch` / `agent=all` branch include shrinks from `[vault_entry, mining_entry, quality_entry]` to `[mining_entry, quality_entry]`; and `vault-manager` is removed from the `workflow_dispatch.inputs.agent` `choice` dropdown (only `all`, `mining-manager`, `quality-matrix-manager` remain). A `repository_dispatch` payload of `client_payload.agent: "vault-manager"` now falls through to the `Unknown agent input` branch and fails the `setup-matrix` step loudly. Stale comments in the file that still named `vault-manager` (the `on:` block intro, the top-level `concurrency` rationale, the matrix `max-parallel: 1` rationale, and the "No submodules" `checkout` comment) are refreshed to reference only the surviving two agents and the generic `vault-manager-mcp` MCP server. The agent itself is not deleted: `agents/vault-manager.md`, `agents/memory/vault-manager/state.json`, `agents/memory/vault-manager/run-log.sepolia.jsonl`, `agents/skills/vault-manager.md`, the `apps/mcps/vault-manager/` MCP server, and the `entryMode: momentum_volume` policy parsing in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` are all left intact, so `node scripts/agent-runner.mjs vault-manager` still works for ad-hoc local invocations. Re-enabling the agent in CI is a deliberate workflow edit (re-add the `vault_entry` definition, the `elif` branch, and the dropdown choice).

### Fixed

- [2026-05-21 17:06 UTC+05:30] **Restore `BasketFactory.createBasket` after the 2026-05-21 VaultAccounting migration, and harden the agent runner against silent cross-agent vault contamination.** Root cause: `script/MigrateVaultAccounting.s.sol` deployed a new `VaultAccounting` (`0xb70ce09018bD05D79AdFB1B78d9F42156804b269`) and pointed `BasketFactory.vaultAccounting` at it, but never re-granted the factory the `wirer` role on the new VA the way the original `script/Deploy.s.sol` does. Because `VaultAccounting.registerVault` is `onlyOwnerOrWirer` (`src/perp/VaultAccounting.sol` `L117-L120,L149`) and `BasketFactory.createBasket` calls it at the end of every deployment (`src/vault/BasketFactory.sol` `L60`), every `create_vault` issued since the migration silently reverted with `"Not authorized"`. Two compounding runner bugs in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` then turned that revert into actively dangerous behaviour: (a) `executeToolCall` ignored the MCP `result.isError === true` convention and recorded reverted writes as if they had succeeded (no `txHash`, nothing pushed to `runSummary.errors`); (b) `extractNewestVaultAddress`, when the agent's `vaultName` didn't match anything in `get_all_vaults`, fell back to `vaults[vaults.length - 1].address` — i.e. the most recently created basket in the factory. Blast radius: the `quality-matrix-manager`'s first run at `2026-05-21T10:17:42Z` got its state.json populated with the `mining-manager`'s vault `0x672371609170aE7E9C8e5e0E08Ec5819D5190c38` (`Minestarters ML Picks`) — verbatim cross-agent ownership stealing. The `mining-manager` ran into bug (a) too but accidentally survived bug (b) because its target vault name already existed in the factory list (it re-attached to the legitimate pre-migration vault). Fixes: (1) `script/MigrateVaultAccounting.s.sol` now calls `VaultAccounting(newVa).setWirer(factory, true)` inside the optional `FACTORY` rewire block (idempotent via the `wirers(factory)` getter); inline NatSpec under step 7 updated. (2) `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` adds a pure helper `recordMcpErrorIfPresent` that surfaces `isError:true` MCP responses into `runSummary.errors`, tags the corresponding `writeAction` with `failed: true`, and — for `create_vault` specifically — arms a new `policyRuntime.createVaultFailedThisRun` flag. The `get_all_vaults` capture branch in `executeToolCall` is gated on that flag so a failed deployment can never be "recovered" by guessing from the factory list, and `extractNewestVaultAddress(content, vaultName)` now returns `null` on a name miss when a `vaultName` was supplied (the legacy "newest entry" fallback still applies for untargeted callers). New unit-test file `scripts/agent-runner-create-vault.test.mjs` (11 tests) covers the null-on-miss path, the case-insensitive name match, the back-compat untargeted lookup, malformed input, `extractVaultAddressFromCreateVaultResponse` on success/error payloads, and four `recordMcpErrorIfPresent` cases (no-op on success, error recording + `createVaultFailedThisRun` arm on `create_vault` failure, no-arm for other write failures, 500-char truncation, null-policyRuntime tolerance). One-shot operator follow-up required to unblock new vault deployments on the live Sepolia stack: `cast send 0xb70ce09018bD05D79AdFB1B78d9F42156804b269 "setWirer(address,bool)" 0x9c80b76abd7d1470ba9e8aaa49d128b0a99dfec4 true --rpc-url "$SEPOLIA_RPC_URL"` (owner-only on the new VA, signed by the deployer wallet `0x36716C8C5D1ae680C78bD0ECc230896556399713`). After the on-chain call lands, delete `agents/memory/quality-matrix-manager/state.json` and `agents/memory/quality-matrix-manager/run-log.sepolia.jsonl` so the next scheduled vault-agent tick redeploys cleanly and persists the agent's own fresh vault address. `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md` row for the new VA updated with the missing-wirer note and a placeholder for the follow-up tx hash.

### Security

- [2026-05-21 20:23 UTC+05:30] **`wire_asset` MCP guard against hallucinated seed prices — server-side live-Yahoo-USD validation at the 20% deviation boundary.** Defence-in-depth follow-up to the 20:02 reseed-AYM/CGNT/LGC/OOR entry. Root cause of that incident was the `quality-matrix-manager` agent skipping its required `yfinance_quote` call (run-log toolCalls array contained zero `yfinance_quote` entries between the 5 `get_quality_company_card` calls and the 5 `wire_asset` calls) and passing hallucinated `seedPriceUsd` values (AYM.AX $4.21 vs live $0.0021; CGNT.V $7.22 vs $0.464; LGC.V $4.13 vs $1.65; OOR.V $3.77 vs $0.0399). `mining-manager`'s run-log shows it DID call `yfinance_quote` correctly and all 12 of its seeds were within ~6% of live USD — so this is a prompt-compliance failure, not a decimals/scaling bug (audited every write path in `AssetWiring.wireAsset`, `OracleAdapter.configureAsset`/`submitPrices`/`seedHistoricalPrices`, `scripts/update-yahoo-finance-prices.js`, the admin oracle page, and the `wire_asset` MCP — all consistently use 8-dec USD raw). Fix moves the same 20% guard one step earlier so a bad call fails fast inside the agent's turn instead of corrupting on-chain state until the next keeper revert:
  - New shared module `apps/shared/yahoo-usd-quote.mjs` exports `fetchLivePriceUsd(symbol)` (FX-converted Yahoo quote with a 60s in-memory FX cache, throws `YahooUnavailableError` on outage), `validateSeedPriceUsd(seedPriceUsd, livePriceUsd, maxBps?)` (pure deviation check, defensive against NaN / zero / negative inputs — no divide-by-zero, no silent pass), and `SEED_PRICE_MAX_DEVIATION_BPS = 2000` (matches `OracleAdapter.configureAsset`'s default `deviationBps` so the MCP rejects exactly the same range the on-chain `_validateDeviation` would).
  - `apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js` refactored to consume `fetchLivePriceUsd` instead of inlining its own `getUsdRate` + `client.quote` block. Pure refactor — `yfinance_quote` return shape (`price`, `priceUsd`, `currency`, `dayChange`, `dayChangePct`, `volume`, `marketCap`, `isAmbiguous`, `candidates`, etc.) is unchanged so the agent prompts that consume it (`mining-manager`, `quality-matrix-manager`, `vault-manager`) need no behavioural change. Per-symbol Yahoo failures still fall through to the inline `error: "Quote failed — check symbol format"` row rather than aborting the whole call.
  - `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js` `wire_asset` handler runs the new guard between `validateWriteSymbolPolicy(symbol)` and `castSend(...)`. Two failure modes: `SEED_PRICE_UNAVAILABLE` (Yahoo unreachable; recovery hint: "skip this pick this run, do NOT retry inside this turn with the same price") and `SEED_PRICE_DEVIATION` (agent's seedPriceUsd outside the 20% band; error message includes both the agent's value and the live `priceUsd`/`currency`/`price` for traceability, plus a recovery hint pointing at the same-turn `yfinance_quote` requirement and explicitly warning against reusing `marketCapUsd` from atlas-ml / atlas-quality). Both use the existing `toolError` helper so they get standard `isError: true` + `redactSecrets` treatment. The tool description string is updated to call the new guard out explicitly so the LLM sees it in the tool catalog without needing to consult the agent prompt.
  - `apps/mcps/vault-manager/package.json` gains `yahoo-finance2: ^3.14.0` (already in the workspace lockfile via `apps/mcps/yfinance`, so CI cache cost is zero).
  - Prompt tightening in `agents/quality-matrix-manager.md` (step 7) and `agents/mining-manager.md` (step 6): the prior bullet-list form is replaced with an explicit **STRICT ORDER** numbered (a)/(b)/(c)/(d) sequence — (a) `yfinance_quote`, (b) skip on `error` / `priceUsd == null` / null `yahooSymbol`, (c) pass the EXACT numeric `priceUsd` (with explicit "NEVER guess" / "NEVER reuse a value from get_quality_company_card / get_ml_top_picks / atlas — those expose marketCapUsd, not per-share USD" warnings AND a callout that `wire_asset` will reject with `SEED_PRICE_DEVIATION`), (d) `wire_asset` call. Both files also gain a new `## Key Rules` bullet repeating the SEED_PRICE_DEVIATION error code + recovery path.
  - Tests: new `apps/mcps/vault-manager/wire-asset-validation.test.mjs` covers 10 cases of the pure `validateSeedPriceUsd` helper — exact match (devBps=0), +5% (ok), exact 20% boundary (ok), +20.5% (reject), today's CGNT.V scale (-94%, reject), `livePriceUsd <= 0` (reject, no divide-by-zero, infinite devBps), `seedPriceUsd <= 0` (reject), NaN inputs (reject both seed and live), custom `maxBps` override (cap 500 bps: 10% rejects, 3% passes), and a constant-pin asserting `SEED_PRICE_MAX_DEVIATION_BPS === 2000` so a future drift from the on-chain default is caught at test time. Run via `node --test apps/mcps/vault-manager/wire-asset-validation.test.mjs`. Existing `node --test scripts/agent-runner-policy.test.mjs` still passes (27 tests).
  - What this deliberately does NOT change: `OracleAdapter` / `AssetWiring` Solidity (the on-chain 20% deviation guard remains the last line of defence — this PR moves the SAME guard one step earlier rather than replacing it); `seedPriceUsd` stays a required positional arg on `wire_asset` (operator picked the validate-and-reject path, not the auto-fetch path, so the agent still has to call `yfinance_quote` — the tool just verifies it did); `scripts/update-yahoo-finance-prices.js` (the keeper batch will still revert wholesale if a stale seed somehow slips through a non-MCP path like a Solidity script — orthogonal follow-up tracked in the 20:02 entry); no retroactive cleanup of today's 4 re-seeded tickers (already correct on-chain from the 20:02 `seedHistoricalPrices` ops).

- [2026-05-21 18:00 UTC+05:30] **Redact the keeper private key from every `cast` failure surface that leaves the runner.** When a `cast send ... --private-key 0x...` invocation reverts, Node's `execFileSync` embeds the full argv — including the secret — into `Error.message`. Verified against Foundry v1.3.1 that `cast send --help` does NOT expose an `env =` clap attribute on `--private-key` (only the keystore-mode options have env support: `ETH_KEYSTORE`, `ETH_KEYSTORE_ACCOUNT`, `ETH_PASSWORD`), so the raw key has to stay on argv unless we restructure the wallet plumbing onto a temp v3 keystore. Instead of doing that, this change adds a comprehensive secret redactor that scrubs the leak at every output boundary so the secret never reaches an artifact, committed file, OpenAI message, or downstream log even if the underlying `cast` error message contains it. New shared module `scripts/lib/redact-secrets.mjs` exports `redactSecrets(text, extraSecrets?)` and `redactSecretsDeep(value, extraSecrets?)`; both replace any literal occurrence of `PRIVATE_KEY` / `ETH_PRIVATE_KEY` / `KEEPER_PRIVATE_KEY` env values (with a 16-char minimum to avoid spurious matches) plus a generic `--private-key (?:0x)?[a-fA-F0-9]{64}` flag pattern. Wired into:
  - `apps/mcps/vault-manager/index.js`: `castCall` / `castSend` consolidated into a `runCast` wrapper that funnels `execFileSync` errors through `redactSecrets` before re-throwing; `toolError(...)` redacts the message before serialising the JSON tool response.
  - `scripts/update-yahoo-finance-prices.js`: the local `runCast` helper now uses piped stdio + try/catch and runs `err.message` / captured stdout / captured stderr through `redactSecrets` before forwarding them to the runner, and the unhandled-error path at the bottom of the script also redacts.
  - `scripts/agent-runner.mjs`: every text path that leaves the runner is redacted — tool-response content + preview before `console.log` and before being pushed into the OpenAI `messages` array, all `runSummary.errors[].error` entries (including the auto-rebalance and memory-failure paths), the `agentSummaryText` printed and stored in `runSummary.summary`, the deep-redacted `runSummary` JSON dump on both success and failure exits, the run-log payload in `memory.appendRunLog` (success and FAILED paths), and the agent-metadata file written by `publishAgentMetadata` (both the latter two are committed back to `main` by the `commit-results` job in `.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml`, which is the highest-risk leak path because GitHub Actions' runner-log masking does **not** apply to files committed back to git).
  - New tests in `scripts/redact-secrets.test.mjs` (11 tests) cover the historical cast-error string, the multi-line revert format, env-var matches, the flag-form fallback when the raw key isn't in any env, untouched non-secret 0x hex (vault addresses + tx hashes), the deep-walking variant, the `extraSecrets` parameter, the short-value safety guard, and the no-op cases.
  - Initial attempt routed the key through `ETH_PRIVATE_KEY` env, which CI immediately rejected with `Error: Error accessing local wallet. Did you set a private key, mnemonic or keystore?` — confirming that Foundry's `RawWalletOpts` doesn't read that env, so the env-only approach was unworkable. The current implementation keeps `--private-key` on argv and treats the redactor as the production safety net (combined with GitHub Actions masking of the literal secret value in runner logs). Recommended follow-up (operator action, NOT performed): rotate `KEEPER_PRIVATE_KEY` because the secret may have been written into past workflow artifacts or commits made by `vault-agent[bot]` before this fix landed.

### Added

- [2026-05-21 17:33 UTC+05:30] **Startup vault-identity guardrail in the agent runner.** Second line of defence after the create-vault contamination fix in commit `00cfb07`. Before either the deterministic auto-rebalance pass or the LLM turn loop is allowed to issue any write against `capturedVaultAddress`, `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` now calls `get_vault_state` on the configured vault and compares the on-chain `BasketVault.name()` to `config.vaultName`. On mismatch the runner throws a detailed `[VAULT IDENTITY]` error that names both the actual on-chain name and the agent's configured name, the offending vault address, and the exact `agents/memory/<agent>/state.json` path the operator should clear to recover; the throw is caught by the existing top-level `catch` in `runAgent`, so the failure is deep-redacted and appended to `run-log.<network>.jsonl` and CI artifacts as a structured `_vault_identity` error. The check is skipped when no vault is deployed yet (`needsNewVault`) or when an agent has no `vaultName` configured (legacy path), and is downgraded to a loud warning under `AGENT_VAULT_OVERRIDE` so operator-driven one-offs still go through. The comparison logic is extracted into a pure helper `verifyVaultNameMatch({ onChainName, expectedName, vaultAddress, agentName })` (case + whitespace insensitive; returns `{ ok, reason?, error?, skipped? }`) and exported via `__agentRunnerInternals`. Covered by 7 new unit tests in `scripts/agent-runner-vault-identity.test.mjs` (exact match, case-insensitive match, surrounding whitespace, mismatch error string content, missing-on-chain-name across `null`/`undefined`/`""`/`"   "`/non-string types, legacy `expectedName` unset, and missing `vaultAddress`/`agentName` placeholder rendering). Had this been live on 2026-05-21, the `quality-matrix-manager` would have aborted on its first contaminated run instead of force-closing three of `mining-manager`'s long legs.
- [2026-05-21 14:56 UTC+05:30] **Quality Matrix mining agent and supporting MCP server.** Added `quality-matrix-manager` — a NEW parallel mining vault agent (`agents/quality-matrix-manager.md`, vault name `Minestarters Quality Matrix`) that picks longs from the analyst's 8-category Quality Matrix (Drilling 35% / Resources 20% / Econ 15% / Met 10% / Permitting 5% / Offtake 5% / Capital Raises 5% / Construction 5%) instead of Atlas ML ranks, and runs alongside the existing `mining-manager` agent for head-to-head ML-vs-Quality-Matrix performance comparison. A new local MCP server `atlas-quality-mcp` (`apps/mcps/atlas-quality/`) reads existing read-only Atlas endpoints (`/dashboard/stocks/baskets`, `/dashboard/company/{ticker}/profile`, `/dashboard/stocks/events`) and computes tiers in JS — Atlas itself is unchanged. The analyst's matrix is transcribed verbatim into `scoring/matrix.json` (52 main signals across 8 categories + the 58-signal drill exploration-vs-resource sub-rubric, with every tier breakpoint, `whatDrivesTheBadge`, `caveatDepositTypeNuance`, `sourceLinks[]`, `workbookAnchors[]`, `provenance` flag (`EMPIRICAL` vs `PUBLISHED_REFERENCE_ONLY`), and `dataQualityWarnings[]` — including the PMET caesium contamination guard on lithium, the single-company i-80 Gold caveat on Pb, the 8-row Saga Metals slice on Fe, and the Athabasca bias note on U). `scoring/matrix.js` exposes one function per category + `scoreDrillProgramType` for the sub-rubric and a top-level `compositeScore` that re-normalises across non-Unknown categories so junior explorers aren't penalised for missing producer-only data, and applies a configurable `provenanceDiscount` (default 0.7) to categories whose every signal is `PUBLISHED_REFERENCE_ONLY`. The `notInWorkbookSchema` signals (Drill Hole Orientation, Drill Spacing, Location Context) always return Unknown with a recovery hint pointing at NI 43-101 extraction. The MCP exposes four tools: `get_quality_top_picks`, `get_quality_company_card`, `get_quality_matrix_definition`, `get_quality_short_candidates` (plus a debug `classify_drill_release_text`). `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` gains a new `entryMode: quality_score` + `entryQualityScoreMin` policy mode (parser accepts and validates them; new `getEligibleQualityScoreAssets` helper; `validatePolicyWriteBatch` uses Quality-Matrix wording; `enforceAutoRebalance` now runs for both `ml_score` and `quality_score` modes, picking the right top-N MCP tool; `computeAutoRebalanceClosures` accepts a `signalLabel` to surface the correct top-N name in close reasons). `publishAgentMetadata` tags quality agents with `signalSource: "atlas-quality"`. The agent reuses the long/short architecture from `mining-manager` (longs from quality top-N, max 1 short per run on names *outside* the top-N that have BOTH a `criticalRedFlag` matrix signal AND a citable bearish headline) and is gated to require every long `justification` to quote the top 2 contributing signals from `get_quality_company_card`. Skill reference at `agents/skills/atlas-quality.md`. Registry entry added in `agents/mcp-servers.json` (`atlas-quality-mcp`); CI matrix in `.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml` extended with `quality_entry` (third agent in the build matrix + new `workflow_dispatch` choice option, still `max-parallel: 1` on the shared `keeper-key-serialized` concurrency group). Test coverage: 24 unit tests in `apps/mcps/atlas-quality/scoring/matrix.test.mjs` verify every analyst anchor (PAAS 1,600m @ 4.0 g/t Au → Exceptional GT + Strong width + Moderate grade; NGEx 335.1m @ 2.25% Cu → Exceptional GT/width + Strong grade; Denison 491m @ 20% U₃O₈ → Exceptional grade/width/GT; Talon 15.2m @ 7.82% Ni → Exceptional grade; the PMET 8.05% caesium contamination guard auto-reclassifies to Unknown; the 58-signal sub-rubric correctly classifies resource-conversion language vs exploration-phase language) + 5 new tests in `scripts/agent-runner-policy.test.mjs` covering `quality_score` parsing, eligibility, validation wording, and auto-rebalance reason text. Docs synced in `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md`, `README.md`, and `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md`.
- [2026-05-21 13:02 UTC+05:30] **Open Positions table deep-links each asset to Yahoo Finance.** In `apps/web/src/components/baskets/positions-table.tsx`, the asset cell of every row (both the desktop table and the mobile stacked card) now renders as an external `target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"` anchor pointing at `https://finance.yahoo.com/quote/<symbol>/` with a small `ExternalLink` icon, when the asset has a tradeable on-chain ticker symbol (e.g. `AAPL`, `BHP.AX`). The symbol comes from `useSupportedOracleAssets().asset.name` and is only used when it does not start with `0x` (matches the existing rule used by the admin oracle page's `OracleAssetCard` and `writeControlsYahooSymbol`); raw `0x...` asset ids without an on-chain symbol stay as plain text. The Yahoo URL helper was extracted from `apps/web/src/app/admin/oracle/page.tsx` into a new shared module `apps/web/src/lib/yahoo-finance.ts` (`yahooFinanceQuoteUrl(symbol)`); the admin page now imports from there. No new data fetching — `name` already flows through `useSupportedOracleAssets`.
- [2026-05-21 13:00 UTC+05:30] **Mining-manager vault now opens news-driven shorts alongside Atlas-ML longs.** The agent runner's `entryDirection` policy in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` (previously locked to `long_only`) now accepts `long_only`, `short_only`, and `long_short`, plus a new `maxNewShortsPerRun` frontmatter field that caps shorts as a subset of the existing combined `maxNewPositionsPerRun` cap (the parser rejects `maxNewShortsPerRun > maxNewPositionsPerRun`, and rejects any non-zero short cap when `entryDirection: long_only`). `validatePolicyWriteBatch` now (a) enforces direction per-call (`long_only` rejects `isLong:false`, `short_only` rejects `isLong:true`), (b) only applies the Atlas-ML / momentum eligibility gate to **long** opens — shorts are LLM-judged from `yfinance_news` context, not from the long-side ranking — and (c) checks the new short cap. The deterministic pre-LLM `enforceAutoRebalance` pass is refactored around a new pure helper `computeAutoRebalanceClosures` (also exported via `__agentRunnerInternals`) so the "dropped from ML top-N" auto-exit only ever closes **long** legs; shorts are entirely owned by the LLM's TP/SL band. The existing `Minestarters ML Picks` vault flips to `entryDirection: long_short, maxNewShortsPerRun: 1`: `agents/mining-manager.md` workflow step 5 now classifies `yfinance_news` headlines bullish/bearish/neutral and sources short candidates from wired oracle assets that are outside the Atlas top-N; step 9 splits open/close into a long lane (Atlas top-N entrants, equal-weight) and a short lane (≤ 50% of long sizing, must cite a bearish headline in `justification`); step 10 requires the `## Thesis` to call out the short rationale separately. New tests in `scripts/agent-runner-policy.test.mjs` cover all three direction values, mixed-batch validation, the short-cap, the `maxNewShortsPerRun` parsing rules, and `computeAutoRebalanceClosures` semantics across modes (`long_only` still cleans up shorts, `long_short` leaves shorts alone, `short_only` cleans up longs and never auto-closes shorts). Docs synced in `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md` (`entryDirection` row + new `maxNewShortsPerRun` row + auto-rebalance description) and `agents/skills/vault-manager.md` (new "Long vs short legs" subsection covering `(vault, assetId, isLong)` keying). No contract or MCP-tool changes — `open_position` / `close_position` already accepted `isLong:false` and the web positions table already renders Long/Short rows.
- [2026-05-21 12:19 UTC+05:30] **`yfinance_news` MCP tool for headline-grounded agent justifications.** `apps/mcps/yfinance/index.js` registers a third tool alongside `yfinance_search` / `yfinance_quote`: `yfinance_news({ symbols[], limitPerSymbol? })` (max 10 symbols, 1-10 headlines each, default 3). It calls `client.search(symbol, { quotesCount: 0, newsCount: N })` from `yahoo-finance2` (already a dependency, no API key required) and returns `[{ symbol, headlines: [{ title, publisher, link, publishedAt (ISO), type, relatedTickers }], error? }]` — per-symbol failures are inlined as `{ headlines: [], error }` so one bad ticker doesn't kill the whole call. `mining-manager` now calls it in step 5 of its workflow and is required to reuse headlines as the `justification` text on `open_position` / `close_position` writes and to cite at least one headline in its `## Thesis` summary when any were returned. Skill reference added in `agents/skills/yfinance.md`; tool table + MCP-server table rows added in `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md`. No new env vars, no new dependencies.
- [2026-05-21 11:15 UTC+05:30] **Mining-themed vault driven by the Atlas ML engine.** Spawned a second autonomous vault agent `agents/mining-manager.md` (vault name `Minestarters ML Picks`) that uses the new `atlas-ml-mcp` MCP server to pull mining-stock predictions from `https://atlas.minestarters.com` and trade them on-chain. The MCP (`apps/mcps/atlas-ml/`) exposes `get_ml_top_picks`, `get_ml_model_info`, `get_ml_basket`, `get_ml_thesis`; each pick is enriched with a derived `yahooSymbol` via an exchange-suffix map (TSXV→.V, TSX→.TO, ASX→.AX, LSE→.L, CSE→.CN, JSE→.JO, NYSE/NASDAQ unsuffixed) so the agent can pipe results straight into `wire_asset` without policy violations. The runner gains a new `entryMode: ml_score` policy mode in `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` (`parseAgentPolicy` accepts `entryMlScoreMin`, `maxTrackedAssets`, `rebalanceMode`; new `getEligibleMlScoreAssets` helper; `validatePolicyWriteBatch` re-uses the existing gate with Atlas-aware wording). A deterministic pre-LLM `enforceAutoRebalance` pass (triggered by `rebalanceMode: track_top_n`) calls a new vault-manager MCP tool `list_open_positions` and closes any leg whose ticker has dropped out of the latest Atlas top-N or whose direction violates `entryDirection`. `.github/workflows/vault-agent.yml` becomes an agent matrix (`vault-manager` + `mining-manager`, `max-parallel: 1` to avoid nonce races on the shared keeper key) and pipes `ATLAS_API_URL` / `ATLAS_API_KEY` secrets through. `publishAgentMetadata` now sets a top-level `signalSource: "atlas-ml"` plus the agent's `entryMode`; the basket detail page renders a new `AtlasMlBadge` ("Powered by Atlas ML") alongside the existing `AiOperatorBadge`, with copy in `apps/web/src/lib/tooltip-copy.ts` (`atlasMl` key). Docs synced in `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md`; `AGENT_DEPLOYMENT_MEMORY.md` records the Atlas API as a `read`-only external dependency. New tests in `scripts/agent-runner-policy.test.mjs` cover `ml_score` parsing, Yahoo-symbol matching, `maxTrackedAssets` capping, and the new violation message.
- [2026-05-21 10:30 UTC+05:30] **Surface AI agent justifications on the basket detail page.** The standalone "Vault Thesis" card on `/baskets/[address]` is replaced by a richer **AI Activity** section (`AiActivitySection` in `apps/web/src/app/baskets/[address]/page.tsx`) that combines the agent identity (`agentName` + `agentDescription`), the Vault Thesis sub-card with an `aiOperator` / `vaultThesis` `InfoTooltip`, a collapsible "Show latest run summary" panel sourced from the new `latestRun.summary` field, and a "Recent Decisions" list rendering the top 5 justified write-tool calls (humanized tool name, relative timestamp, justification text, tx link). The hero `AiOperatorBadge` now also renders an inline help tooltip. Vault History rows now show a chip with the AI agent name + tool ("AI: vault-manager · Allocate To Perp") above the justification italic so investors can see which action and operator each blurb belongs to. `scripts/agent-runner.mjs` `publishAgentMetadata` now (a) dedupes `recentActions` by `txHash` so re-runs don't push older justified actions out, (b) raises the cap from 20 to 100 (override via `AGENT_METADATA_ACTION_LIMIT`), (c) tags each action with `agentName` and `runId`, and (d) emits a top-level `latestRun: { runId, finishedAt, summary }`. The `AgentAction` / `AgentMetadata` types in `apps/web/src/hooks/useAgentMetadata.ts` gain the matching optional fields (all back-compat). Documentation synced in `docs/AGENTS_FRAMEWORK.md` and `apps/web/public/agent-metadata/README.md`.
